# SOURCE_FILE: CHEY-2023-11-07_TotalExtraction_66841215.pdf
RECORD OF PROCEEDINGS FOR THE GOVERNING BODY 
OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE 
Office of City Clerk 
 
November 13, 2023  
 
 
The Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne met in regular session on this date beginning 
at 6:00 p.m. in Council Chambers and via electronic conference meeting.  Present were: MAYOR 
– Patrick Collins, COUNCIL MEMBERS – Dr. Michelle Aldrich, Bryan Cook, Ken Esquibel, 
Richard Johnson, Pete Laybourn, Dr. Mark Rinne, Scott Roybal, Tom Segrave and Jeff White. 
Also present: Kris Jones, City Clerk.  The pledge of allegiance was recited. 
 
Consent Agenda.  (All agenda items listed with the designation of [CA] are considered to 
be routine items by the governing body and will be enacted by one motion. There will be no 
separate discussion on these items unless a member of the governing body so requests and support 
by two other members is received.  Any item removed from the Consent Agenda will be considered 
in its normal sequence on the agenda.  Upon request by Dr. Aldrich, and pursuant to support 
received by Mr. Segrave and Mr. Esquibel, item #16 was removed from the Consent Agenda. Upon 
request by Mr. Laybourn, and pursuant to support received by Dr. Aldrich and Mr. Segrave, item 
#17 was removed from the Consent Agenda.  Upon request by Mr. Cook, and pursuant to support 
received by Mr. Roybal and Mr. Segrave, item #27(e) was removed from the Consent Agenda.  
Upon request by Mr. White, and pursuant to support received by Dr. Aldrich and Mr. Esquibel, 
item #28(a) was removed from the Consent Agenda.  Mr. White moved to approve remaining 
items, seconded by Dr. Aldrich.  Motion carried. Voting “yes” – all members of the governing 
body. 
 
[CA] 
Minutes from Regular Meeting of the Governing Body on October 23, 2023.  Motion (per 
Consent Agenda) carried. 
 
Mayor Collins opened a public hearing to receive comments and input for a bar and grill 
application filed with the City Clerk’s Office for Wyoming Horse Racing LLC d/b/a Horse Palace, 
1802 Dell Range Blvd. Suite 3, Cheyenne, WY.  Public comment was made by: Steve Jimenez, 
General Manager, Wyoming Horse Racing.  Comments were made by the following members of 
the governing body: Mayor Collins, Mr. Cook, Mr. Laybourn and Mr. Roybal.  There being no 
further comments or input, and purpose of the hearing having been met, Mayor Collins closed the 
hearing and referred the application to Finance Committee. 
 
 
Mayor Collins opened a public hearing to receive comments and input to determine 
whether compliance of annexation conditions required by Wyoming Statute §15-1-402 exist for 
the City-initiated annexation of 51 parcels of land totaling 100.56 acres comprised of various tracts 
of land situate in northwest Cheyenne generally located north of Dell Range Boulevard and west 
of Powderhouse Road.  Public comment was made by: April Brimmer Kunz, Jack Knudson, Gloria 
Milmont, Abe DeHerrera, Booyong Kim and Bill Weaver.  Comments were made by the following 
members of the governing body: Dr. Aldrich and Mayor Collins. There being no further comments 
or input, and purpose of the hearing having been met, Mayor Collins closed the hearing. 
 
 

 
 ORDINANCE –3rd READING – Creating Section 12.04.240, Mobile Storage Containers 
on Public Right-of-Way; Permit Required, of Chapter 12.04, Streets, Sidewalks and Driveways 
Generally, of Title 12, Streets, Sidewalks and Public Places, of the Municipal Code of the City of 
Cheyenne, Wyoming and making a conforming amendment as specified. (Postponed from October 
23, 2023). Reporting for Finance Committee, Mr. White moved to approve on third and final 
reading, seconded by Dr. Aldrich.  Mr. White moved to postpone to November 27, 2023, seconded 
by Dr. Aldrich. Motion to postpone carried. Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body 
with the exception of Mr. Cook voting “no”.  Comment was made by the following member of the 
governing body: Mr. Cook. 
 
 
ORDINANCE –3rd READING – Amending Section 2.04.020, Mayor-Election-Term of 
Office, of Chapter 2.04, Mayor and City Council, of Title 2, Administration and Personnel, of the 
Municipal Code of the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, specifying the employment status of persons 
hired in the Mayor’s office. Reporting for Public Services Committee, Mr. Cook moved to approve 
on third and final reading, seconded by Mr. Segrave. Motion carried. Voting “yes” – all members 
of the governing body.  Comments were made by the following members of the governing body: 
Dr. Aldrich, Mayor Collins and Mr. Cook. (#4529) 
 
 
ORDINANCE –2nd READING – Amending the Official Zoning map of the City of 
Cheyenne changing the zoning classification from MUB Mixed-Use Business Emphasis to CB 
Community Business for land located at Lot 2, Block 1, Sunnyside Addition, 10th Filing.  Mr. 
Cook moved to approve on second reading, seconded by Mr. Segrave.  Motion carried.  Voting 
“yes” – all members of the governing body. 
 
 
ORDINANCE –2nd READING – Amending Subsections 6.6.3.b.3, 6.7.5.c, and 6.9.3.e of 
the Unified Development Code (UDC), changing the building façade design requirements for 
multifamily residential development.  Mr. Cook moved to approve on second reading, seconded 
by Mr. White.  Mr. Cook moved to postpone to November 27, 2023, seconded by Mr. Segrave.  
Motion to postpone carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body.  Comments were 
made by the following members of the governing body: Dr. Aldrich, Mayor Collins, Mr. Cook, 
Mr. Laybourn and Dr. Rinne. 
 
ORDINANCE –1st READING – Amending the Official Zoning Map of the City of

# SOURCE_FILE: CHEY-2023-11-07_TotalExtraction_77817941.pdf
 
ORDINANCE NO. _________ 
 
ENTITLED: "AN ORDINANCE CREATING SECTION 12.04.240, MOBILE STORAGE 
CONTAINERS ON PUBLIC RIGHT-OF-WAY; PERMIT REQUIRED, OF 
CHAPTER 
12.04, 
STREETS, 
SIDEWALKS 
AND 
DRIVEWAYS 
GENERALLY, OF TITLE 12, STREETS, SIDEWALKS AND PUBLIC 
PLACES, OF THE MUNICIPAL CODE OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE, 
WYOMING, AND MAKING A CONFORMING AMENDMENT AS 
SPECIFIED." 
 
BE IT ORDAINED BY THE GOVERNING BODY OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE, 
WYOMING: 
 
Section 1.  That Section 12.04.240, Mobile Storage Containers on Public Right-Of-Way; 
Permits Required, of Chapter 12.04, Streets, Sidewalks and Driveways Generally, of Title 12, 
Streets, Sidewalks and Public Places, of the Cheyenne City Code are hereby created to read as 
follows: 
 
12.04.240 - Mobile storage containers on public right-of-way; permit required. 
 
A. An encroachment permit shall be required for any mobile storage container that will occupy 
any part of the street or will otherwise be placed on the public right-of-way. No mobile 
storage container shall occupy any part of the sidewalk located within the public right of 
way. 
 
B. Any person wanting to place a mobile storage container on the right-of-way shall apply to 
the city engineer for an encroachment permit no later than ten (10) business days prior to 
the placement of the mobile storage container at a particular location. The permit 
application shall identify the proposed location of the mobile storage container to be placed 
on the public right-of-way, the name and contact information for the property owner or 
tenant who will use the container, the name and contact information of the business through 
which the mobile storage container will be obtained and any other information specified 
by the city engineer. The permit shall be displayed on the exterior of the mobile storage 
container at all times. 
 
C. In addition to the information required to be submitted under subsection (B) of this section, 
a permit applicant shall submit adequate proof of liability insurance for the placement of 
the mobile storage container. Liability coverage under the insurance shall be in an amount 
determined by the city engineer and the insurance shall be maintained during the entirety 
of the time the mobile storage container is to be placed on the public right-of-way. 
 
D. An initial mobile storage container encroachment permit shall not require the payment of 
any permit fee but upon approval by the city engineer of the permit application shall 
authorize the placement of the mobile storage container for a period of not more than ninety 
(90) days. An encroachment permit under this section may be renewed not more than two 
(2) times for any specified location and for no more than a combined total of one hundred 
12:00 pm, Nov 21, 2023

 
 
Page 2 of 4 
eighty (180) days in any consecutive twelve (12) month period. An encroachment permit 
under this section may be renewed as follows: 
 
1. A first permit renewal shall require payment of a one hundred and fifty dollars 
($150.00) permit renewal fee, which upon approval by the city engineer and payment 
shall authorize the continued placement of the mobile storage container for an 
additional period of sixty (60) days from the date of the initial permit renewal 
expiration; and 
 
2. A second permit renewal shall require payment of a seven hundred fifty dollars 
($750.00) permit renewal fee, which upon approval by the city engineer and payment 
shall authorize the continued placement of the mobile storage container for an 
additional period of thirty (30) days from the date of the first permit renewal expiration. 
 
E. No mobile storage container that is or will be placed in a residentially zoned area shall be 
delivered or removed for transport between the hours of 7:00 p.m. and 6:00 a.m. 
 
F. The vertical stacking of two (2) or more mobile storage containers and stacking of any 
other materials on top of a portable storage container is prohibited. 
 
G. The city engineer, in his or her discretion, may approve, conditionally approve or deny any 
mobile storage container encroachment permit application. All mobile storage container 
encroachment permits are revocable at the discretion of the city engineer. If in the 
judgement of the city engineer, the public interest or convenience will be served by removal 
or relocation of a mobile storage container from the public right-of-way, the city engineer 
shall modify, condition or revoke the encroachment permit accordingly. Any permit 
revoked under this subsection shall entitle the former permit holder to a pro rata refund 
based on the remaining number of days of the permit but for the revocation by the city 
engineer.  
 
H. Notwithstanding the one hundred eighty (180) day limitation imposed under subsection 
(D) of this section and for good cause shown, the city engineer may grant a permittee 
waiver, authorizing additional time during which the permittee's mobile storage container 
may be placed on the public right-of-way. Any waiver granted under this subsection shall 
not exceed sixty (60) days and shall be evidenced through the issuance of a permit waiver 
which shall be displayed in accordance with subsection (A) of this section. 
 
I. Any person found in violation of this section shall be guilty of a misdemeanor punishable 
by a fine of not more than seven hundred fifty dollars ($750.00). Each day of a continuing 
violation shall constitute a separate offense. 
 
J. As used in this section, "mobile storage container" means a moveable container that is 
temporarily placed on the public right-of-way adjacent to a property and is used or intended 
to be used by the property owner or tenant for short-term storage of items, including but 

 
 
Page 3 of 4 
not limited to clothing, equipment, materials, merchandise or household or office fixtures 
or furnishings. For the purposes of this section, "mobile storage container" shall include 
roll off dumpsters. 
 
Section 2.  That Section 12.04.080, Construction and repair—Permits required—Fees—
Exception, of Chapter 12.04, Streets, Sidewalks and Driveways Generally, of Title 12, Streets, 
Sidewalks and Public Places, of the Cheyenne City Code are hereby amended to read as follows: 
 
12.04.080 - Construction and repair; —Permits permits required; —Feesfees; —Exception 
exception. 
 
A. No person may construct, remove or repair any sidewalk, curb and gutter, driveway, 
roadway or street, or perform any other work within the public right-of-way without first 
obtaining a right-of-way encroachment permit. Right-of-way encroachment permit and 
plan review fees for work in the public right-of-way shall be based on the valuation of the 
work and paid in accordance with the schedule of permit fees contained in Section 
15.08.030. If the work to be done in within the right-of-way is in conjunction with a 
building permit, a separate right-of-way encroachment permit is required. 
 
B. This section shall not apply to the placement of mobile storage containers under Section 
12.04.240 of the city code. 
 
B. C. 
Exception. None, unless waived specifically by the city engineer. 
 
Section 3.  Sections, paragraphs, sentences, clauses, and phrases of this ordinance are 
severable. If any section, paragraph, sentence, clause, or phrase of this ordinance is declared 
unconstitutional or otherwise unlawful, such declaration shall not affect any of the remaining 
sections, paragraphs, sentences, clauses, and phrases.  
 
Section 4.  That this ordinance shall be in full force and effect upon approval and 
publication. 
 
 
FIRST READING: 
 
 
____________________________________ 
 
SECOND READING:  
 
____________________________________ 
 
 

 
 
Page 4 of 4 
THIRD AND FINAL READING: 
____________________________________ 
 
 
_________________________________________ 
 
 
 
 
 
 
PATRICK COLLINS, MAYOR 
 
(SEAL) 
 
 
ATTEST: 
 
____________________________________ 
KRISTINA F. JONES, CITY CLERK 
 
 
Published:___________________ 
Wyoming Tribune-Eagle 


--- END OF FILE ---

ORDINANCE NO. ________ 
ENTITLED: "AN ORDINANCE AMENDING THE OFFICIAL ZONING MAP OF THE 
CITY OF CHEYENNE CHANGING THE ZONING CLASSIFICATION 
FROM MUB MIXED-USE BUSINESS EMPHASIS TO CB COMMUNITY 
BUSINESS FOR LAND LOCATED AT LOT 2, BLOCK 1, SUNNYSIDE 
ADDITION, 10TH FILING." 
 
BE IT ORDAINED BY THE GOVERNING BODY OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE, 
WYOMING: 
Section 1. That the owner(s) of the property described herein has (have) applied for a 
zoning map amendment for said property in accordance with Section 2.2.1, Zoning Map 
Amendment, of the Cheyenne Unified Development Code ("UDC").
Section 2.  That on October 2, 2023, the City of Cheyenne Planning Commission 
conducted a public hearing, reviewed the proposed zoning map amendment that is the subject of 
this ordinance, and recommended approval of said zoning map amendment.
Section 3.  That the Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne finds that the zoning map 
amendment that is the subject of this ordinance meets the criteria specified in Section 2.2.1.d of 
the UDC. 
Section 4.  That, in accordance with Section 2.2.1, Zoning Map Amendment, Section 5.1.2,
Zoning Districts Established, and Section 5.1.3, Official Zoning Map, of the UDC, the 
aforementioned application is hereby approved, and the zoning classification of the real property 
described as Lot 2, Block 1, Sunnyside Addition, 11th Filing, Cheyenne, Wyoming, is hereby 
changed to CB Community Business.
Section 5. That the City Engineer is hereby authorized and directed to alter and amend the 
Official Zoning Map as described in Section 5.1.3 of the UDC to comply with the zoning map 
amendment as described in Section 4 above when this ordinance takes effect in accordance with 
Section 7 below. 
Section 6. Sections, paragraphs, sentences, clauses and phrases of this ordinance are 
severable. If any section, paragraph, sentence, clause, or phrase of this ordinance is declared 
unconstitutional, such unconstitutionality shall not affect any of the remaining sections, 
paragraphs, sentences, clauses, and phrases. 
Section 7. That this ordinance shall be in full force and effect upon its approval and 
publication.

STAFF REPORT
FILE:
UDC-23-120: Cleveland and US 30 Zone Change 
REQUEST:
A request to rezone approximately 2.02 acres of land from MUB Mixed-
use Business to CB Community Business.
LOCATION:
North of Pershing Boulevard between Cleveland Avenue and US 30
AGENT:
OWNER:
Shane Hansen
Ramirez, Daniel Et Ux
ZONING:
MUB Mixed-use Business
FUTURE LAND USE:
Mixed-use Commercial
PURPOSE:
Rezoning to CB to allow storage with fewer design standards.
PREPARED BY:
Seth Lloyd, Senior Planner
Planning and Development Department
(Phone) 307-637-6282
(Fax) 307-637-6366

UDC-23-120
October 2, 2023
RECOMMENDED MOTION: 
 
Move to approve the rezoning of Lot 2, Block 1, Sunnyside Addition, 10th Filing to CB Community 
Business, noting that the project meets the review criteria for approval. 
 
APPLICABLE CITY CODE SECTION(S) AND PLANS: 
 
 
UDC 2.2.1 Zoning Map Amendment 
 
UDC Article 5 
 
PlanCheyenne 
 
BACKGROUND: 
 
This zone change application proposes changing the zoning from MUB Mixed-use Business to CB Community 
Business. The land is currently vacant. The purpose of the proposed zone change is to allow for a storage use 
with more appropriate design standards.  
 
SURROUNDING LAND USE AND ZONING: 
 
 
ANALYSIS OF APPLICATION CONFORMANCE WITH REVIEW CRITERIA: 
 
Should the Planning Commission wish to recommend approval of the Zoning Map Amendment, the 
Commission must make the following findings required in UDC 2.2.1.d. A detailed analysis is below: 
 
1) The application is in accordance with the development policies and physical patterns 
identified in the Comprehensive Plan.  (UDC 2.2.1.d.1) 
 
The Future Land Use designation for this property is Mixed-use Commercial. 
Commercial category is intended to promote a range of land uses, with primarily retail, office, 
light industrial, and live/work 
 plazas and/or open space 
should also be part of 
 
 
Policies in the comprehensive plan encourage commercial development near activity centers, 
and the intersection of College and Pershing is considered an activity center by the plan.   
 
The proposed zone change for the subject property complies with this review criterion.

MUB Mixed-use Business

service uses

UDC-23-120
October 2, 2023
2)
The application is compatible with nearby property, specifically considering existing zoning, 
the Comprehensive Plan, and any other official plans and policies created under the guidance 
of that plan for these areas; (UDC 2.2.1.d.2) 
 
There is CB zoning to the south of this property. The surrounding area consists of a mix of 
different commercial, residential, assembly, and service uses. The CB zone would be compatible 
with the nearby properties as it is a commercial zone with slightly different standards than the 
MUB zone.   
 
The proposed zone change for the subject property complies with this review criterion. 
   
3) The suitability of the subject property for development according to the standards and uses of 
the existing zoning district, compared to the suitability of the subject property for 
development according to the standards and uses of the proposed zoning district (UDC 
2.2.1.d.3) 
  
The surrounding area better matches the expected development patterns of CB zoning. The 
MUB zone requires buildings to be set closer to the streets and have a certain amount of 
windows and other building design elements, whereas the CB zone pushes buildings further 
from the street and requires fewer building design elements. The CB zone is also meant for 
more vehicle-oriented areas which would be the case for US 30.   
   
The proposed zone change for the subject property complies with this review criterion. 
 
4) The ability of the subject property and surrounding vicinity to be developed according to the 
Subdivision Standards in Article 4 considering the proposed zoning (UDC 2.2.1.d.4) 
 
This is in an area with an existing street network 
limitations on US 30. There are infill and regional transportation route limitations on the 
property no matter what zone is selected. The CB zone generally anticipates larger blocks and is 
better suited to the likely development patterns in this situation.  
 
The proposed zone change for the subject property complies with this review criterion. 
 
5) The ability of the application to support the Intent and Applicability statements of the 
proposed zoning district and achieve the Design Objectives of any specific standards. (UDC 
2.2.1.d.5) 
 
The CB District is intended for Retail, Employment, Service and Civic uses that may serve a 
number of residential neighborhoods or other districts in meeting daily needs, discretionary 
needs, or other business functions. The district regulations are designed to encourage these 
uses, while also protecting the abutting and nearby areas.  
 
The CB District is applicable in areas where moderate- or large-scale business uses are 
appropriate, specifically larger Mixed-use Commercial Activity Centers, Community/Regional 
Activity Centers, or Community Business areas of the Comprehensive Plan. 
 
This property is close to US 30 and has a more low-density, spread-out development pattern, 
better meeting the Intent and design objectives of the CB zone, than those of the MUB zone.  
 

UDC-23-120
October 2, 2023
The proposed zone change for the subject property complies with this review criterion.
    
6) The ability of all potential future development to meet all applicable standards for 
development in the proposed zoning district, including other development options if any 
specific development proposed in association with the application is not executed after 
approval. (UDC 2.2.1.d.6) 
 
The specific proposal for this project is a storage facility. However if that project is not executed 
after approval, the CB zone offers a wide variety of potential auto-oriented uses at this site 
(including apartments).   
 
The proposed zone change for the subject property complies with this review criterion. 
 
7) The review and recommendations of other reviewing bodies. (UDC 2.2.1.d.7) 
 
No major concerns were raised by other revieing bodies. 
 
The proposed zone change for the subject property complies with this review criterion. 
 
8) Whether the application will harm the public health, safety, or general welfare. (UDC 
2.2.1.d.8) 
 
City staff is satisfied that this proposed zone change would not adversely affect the public 
health, safety, or general welfare. 
 
The proposed zone change for the subject property complies with this review criterion. 
 
CONFORMANCE WITH APPLICABLE PLANS: 
 
The project is located in an area covered by the following plans: 
 
PlanCheyenne 
Conformance with PlanCheyenne is covered by review criterion 1.     
 
STAFF REVIEW: 
 
Through the review process no comments were received. 
 
CONCLUSION: 
 
Staff is satisfied that the application meets the review criteria set forth in UDC 2.2.1.(d).  The proposed 
CB zone is consistent with the comprehensive plan and is appropriate based on the existing conditions 
and potential development patterns for the area.  
 
ALTERNATIVES: 
 
1. Recommend approval of the zoning amendment as submitted by the applicant (Staff 
Recommendation).  
2. Recommend approval of the zoning amendment subject to conditions or modifications.  

UDC-23-120
October 2, 2023
3.
Recommend denial of the zoning amendment based on findings of denial. The Planning 
Commission must conclude that the project does not meet all the review criteria. As part of the 
motion, findings for denial must be stated. 
4. Postpone the zoning amendment until issues identified during the meeting can be resolved. 
 
General Information Regarding Alternatives: 
 
If the Commission acts contrary 
Commission shall cite specific reasons (based on the review criteria) for approval or denial. 
 
STAFF RECOMMENDATION: 
 
Staff recommends approval of the rezoning of the property from MUB to CB.

MEMO 
To: 
Honorable Mayor Patrick Collins and Members of the City Council 
From: 
Seth Lloyd, Senior Planner 
Subject: A Text Amendment amending subsections 6.6.3.b.3, 6.7.5, and 6.9.3.e of the Unified 
Development Code (UDC), changing building façade design requirements for multifamily 
residential development   
Date: 
October 18, 2023 
 
Background:  
The item for consideration is a UDC Text Amendment. This is approved by Ordinance.    
 
The proposed Governing Body review schedule is as follows: 
• 
Introduction and Referral  
10/23/2023 
• 
Public Services Committee  
11/7/2023 
• 
Second reading  
 
11/13/2023 
• 
Public Services Committee  
11/21/2023 
• 
Third and final reading   
11/27/2023 
 
Planning Commission Recommendation:  
The Planning Commission heard this item at their October 16, 2023 meeting and voted 3-2 and 3-1 to 
recommend the Governing Body approve this application as amended.  
 
The Planning Commission had a long discussion on the appropriateness of materials requirements and 
color requirements. After discussion, the Planning Commission divided the question into three sections. 
The changes to required windows in 6.7.5.b Façade Openings was postponed to a subsequent meeting. 
The changes to the various materials sections were amended to recommend the removal of all required 
materials for multifamily development, but to require two different colors at no minimum percentage. 
This amended motion passed 3-2. The changes to the requirements for carports was recommended for 
approval as proposed. This motion passed by a vote of 3-1 (one Planning Commission member recused 
themselves from the vote on this item). 
 
The Ordinance attached to this submittal reflects the recommendations of the Planning Commission, 
and does not include amendments to 6.7.5.b Façade openings. 
 
The Planning Commission Staff report is attached and has not been changed.

2. Planning Commission Staff Report

ENTITLED: "AN ORDINANCE AMENDING SUBSECTIONS 6.6.3.b.3, 6.7.5.c, AND 
6.9.3.e OF THE UNIFIED DEVELOPMENT CODE (UDC), CHANGING 
BUILDING FAÇADE DESIGN REQUIREMENTS FOR MULTIFAMILY 
RESIDENTIAL DEVELOPMENT." 
 
 
BE IT ORDAINED BY THE GOVERNING BODY OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE, 
WYOMING: 
 
Section 1.  That the Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne finds that the text amendment 
that is the subject of this ordinance meets the criteria specified in Section 2.4.1.d of the Unified 
Development Code. 
 
Section 2.  That on October 16, 2023, the Planning Commission conducted a public 
hearing, reviewed the proposed text amendment, and recommended approval of said text 
amendment. 
 
Section 3.  That Subsection 6.6.3.b.3 Materials for Multi-dwelling buildings, of Subsection 
6.6.3.b Building Design, of Subsection 6.6.3 General Residential Design, of Section 6.6 
Residential Design Standards, of Article 6 Design Regulations, of the Unified Development Code 
of the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, is hereby repealed and replaced as set out in this section. 
 
3. Façade Design. All attached dwelling and multi-dwelling building types shall be 
constructed according to the following façade standards. All buildings shall provide 
two distinct colors. Carports, stand-alone canopies, or other structures which only 
consist of vertical support posts shall not have any one color cover more than 70% of 
the post. 
 
Table 6-18: Reserved 
 
Section 4.  That Subsection 6.7.5.c Building Materials, of Subsection 6.7.5 Building 
Design, of Section 6.7 Small Scale Commercial and Mixed-use Design Standards, of Article 6 
Design Regulations, of the Unified Development Code of the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, is 
hereby amended as set out in this section. 
 
c. Building Materials. All buildings and accessory buildings over 200 square feet shall be 
constructed according to the following material standards. Percentage requirements for 
materials exclude windows or other openings. Buildings which are more than 50% 
residential dwelling units (by square footage) are exempt from the material requirements 
but are required to provide at least two colors on the building. Carports, stand-alone 
canopies, or other structures which only consist of vertical support posts in the area 
between 2’ and 8’ above grade and are less than 2 stories in height shall be exempt from 
the materials requirements of this section if at least two distinct colors are provided on the 
post. No one color shall cover more than 70% of the post.

trim materials covering between 5% and 10% of the building facades.

Planning and Development Office 
2101 O’Neil Avenue, Suite 202, Cheyenne WY 82001 
(Phone) 307-637-6282   (Fax) 307-637-6366 
 
 
 
 
PLANNING COMMISSION  
OCTOBER 16, 2023 
STAFF REPORT  
 
REQUEST: 
 
A Text Amendment amending subsections 6.6.3.b.3, 6.7.5, and 6.9.3.e of the 
Unified Development Code (UDC), changing building façade design requirements for 
multifamily residential development   
CASE NUMBER:  
PLTA-23-2 
PREPARED BY: 
Seth Lloyd, Senior Planner, Planning and Development Department 
 
 
RECOMMENDED MOTION:  
 
Move to recommend the Governing Body approve text amendments to the Unified Development Code 
subsections 6.6.3.b.3, 6.7.5, and 6.9.3.e as outlined in this staff report and recommended by staff, noting 
the requests meets the review criteria of UDC 2.4.1.d.   
 
 
BACKGROUND: 
 
The City’s Unified Development Code (UDC) was adopted by the Governing Body on January 23, 2012, by 
Ordinance Number 3943 with an effective date of April 30, 2012.  The Unified Development Code is the 
regulatory document carrying out the vision of PlanCheyenne and is reviewed annually pursuant to UDC 
1.1.7 [Annual Review Requirement] to determine if any particular standard or regulation is timely, 
relevant and adequately addresses current market conditions. In addition to the annual review, the 
Planning and Development Director and City Engineer are authorized under UDC 2.4.1.b.3 to bring 
forward text amendments on their own initiative.  
 
The proposed text amendment is to change various sections of code regarding façade design 
requirements for multifamily buildings. Multifamily buildings in the residential and mixed-use zones 
follow the design requirements found in 6.6. However, multifamily buildings are also called ‘Urban Lofts’ 
in certain scenarios, applying the design requirements of 6.7 in these cases; and multifamily buildings are 
allowed via conditional use in the commercial zones, which would apply the design standards of 6.9. 
Various zone districts also allow mixed-use buildings, which could be used as a majority multifamily and 
in some cases may have multifamily on the ground floor. The code amendment proposes removing the 
requirement for brick and stone for multifamily buildings and carports. It also proposes removing a display 
window requirement for multifamily construction and a few minor typographical edits to the affected 
sections. 
 
  
  
  
  
  
  
  
  Planning and Development Department 
2101 O’Neil Avenue, Suite 202, Cheyenne WY 82001 
(Phone) 307-637-6282 
(Fax) 307-637-6366 

PLTA-23-2  
 
 
October 16, 2023 
 
The current code requires unpainted brick or stone on 30% of the building façades, and does not allow 
these to be replaced with simulated brick or stone materials. The current code also requires a minimum 
amount of display windows on the ground floor for 6.7 design standards, and all three types of design 
standards have been applied to carports in multifamily developments.  
The affordable housing task force recommended that these items be modified such that brick or stone 
was not required on multifamily structures. A separate request for a text amendment also specifically 
requested that carports and other canopies not be required to provide brick or stone on the support 
pillars. 
 
The requirement for brick and stone is largely aesthetic, as such materials are only a veneer on the 
materials actually used in construction and have little effect on the structure. Brick and stone may be 
more durable than other materials that could be selected for veneer. As a largely aesthetic regulation, 
Staff proposes changing the code to require two distinct materials and two distinct colors, instead of 
requiring brick or stone.  
 
Staff’s assumption is that the requirement of two different materials and colors (without brick and stone) 
is acceptable to the community. A small outreach event with the public substantiated Staff’s assumption:  
apartment buildings with two different colors without brick and stone were preferred over buildings with 
brick and stone (a detailed breakdown of the event is found in the attachments). 
 
It has been said that the market will charge whatever they can for apartment units, and therefore the cost 
of construction does not factor into play as much for rental rates. However, the cost of construction will 
factor into the overall number of units built, and typically a greater supply of units will make rents lower. 
  
PROPOSED AMENDMENTS: 
Subsection 6.6.3.b.3 Materials for Multi-dwelling buildings  
(of 6.6.3.b Building Design, of 6.6.3 General Residential Design, of 6.6 Residential Design Standards):

materials exclude windows or other openings. . Carports, stand-alone canopies, or other 
structures which only consist of vertical support posts in the area between 2’ and 8’ above 
grade and are less than 2 stories in height shall be exempt from the materials requirements 
of this section if at least two distinct colors are provided on the post. No one color shall 
cover more than 70% of the post.

covering at least 30%90% of the building facades. No one primary material may cover 
more than 70% of the facades. No one color shall cover more than 70% of the facades 
(colors shall be distinctly different, if there is a debate over whether the proposed colors 
are distinctly different, an applicant may appeal the Director’s determination to the 
Board of Adjustment). 
(b) Secondary Materials. Buildings may have a secondary material covering up to 70% of the 
façades. 
(c)(b)

accent or trim materials covering between 5% and 10% of the building facades. The 
accent shall be a separate material from the primary materials utilized on the building.

the Table 6-18.

Subsection 6.7.5.b Façade Openings  
(of 6.7.5 Building Design, of 6.7 Small Scale Commercial and Mixed-use Design Standards): 
 
b. Façade Openings. Each facade facing a street shall have openings at street level and upper levels 
according to the following: 
1. Between 60% and 90% of all street-level facades between 2’ and 10’ above grade shall be 
transparent with views to the interior of the building. No window starting at a level of 
greater than 3.5’ above the street level should be included in the calculation. Where the 
interior operation or program of the building warrants screening from the street, a closed 
display window of at least 2.5 feet deep may be substituted along up to 50% of the linear 
distance of the façade. Any section of a building with residential dwelling units on the floor 

PLTA-23-2  
 
 
October 16, 2023 
 
that predominately covers the space between 2’ and 10’ above grade are exempt from this 
requirement in the areas where the residential units are located. 
2. No more than 20’ of building frontage shall exist at the street level without window or door 
openings. On secondary or support streets that are not the primary pedestrian and retail 
streets of the Center, the Director may approve facades with greater than 20’ of building 
frontage without window and door openings. 
3. Upper level openings shall occupy between 20% and 50% of the façade and consist of 
punched and regularly spaced openings to create a rhythm and pedestrian scale for the 
façade. 
4. Corner buildings with two street frontages may designate one frontage as the primary 
frontage; however, at least the first 30’ of secondary frontage from the corner shall meet 
the above minimum opening requirements.

REVIEW CRITERIA: 
 
Review of a UDC text amendment shall be measured by the following three (3) criteria of Section 2.4.1.d: 
 
1. COMPREHENSIVE PLAN:  
“The amendment is in accordance with general goals and policies of the Comprehensive Plan, or 
is necessary to address an emerging issue not anticipated in the Comprehensive Plan and is not 
inconsistent with the plan.” 
 

PLTA-23-2  
 
 
October 16, 2023 
 
The Comprehensive plan encourages the development of workforce housing and housing for 
Seniors (Principle 2). PlanCheyenne encourages workforce housing to be made of high-quality 
materials, and with architectural details that are functional and attractive (Policy 2.5.c). Another 
policy states that design standards should be created to address should be high-quality, but no 
specific materials are mentioned. 
    
2. STATE STATUTORY REQUIREMENTS:  
The amendment is consistent with the Wyoming Statutes, and specifically the Municipal Planning 
and Zoning statutes. The following Articles are all listed verbatim from Wyoming State Statute. 
 
WYO 15-1-601. 
Regulations; scope and purpose; uniformity within authorized districts; 
to follow plan; objectives. 
  
(a)  The governing body of any city or town, by ordinance, may: 
(i)  Regulate and restrict the: 
  
 
(A)  Height, number of stories and size of buildings and other structures; 
(B)  Percentage of lot that may be occupied; 
  
 
(C)  Size of yards, courts and other open spaces; 
(D)  Density of population; and 
  
 
(E) Location and use of buildings, structures and land for trade, industry, 
residence or other purposes. 
  
(ii)  Establish setback building lines. 
  
(b)  The governing body may divide the city or town into districts of such number, shape and area 
as it deems necessary, and within those districts it may regulate and restrict the erection, 
construction, reconstruction, alteration, repair or use of buildings, structures or land. 
  
(c)  Regulations may differ from one (1) district to another but shall be uniform for each class or 
kind of buildings within a district. 
  
(d)  All regulations shall be made: 
(i)  In accordance with a comprehensive plan and designed to: 
  
 
(A)  Lessen congestion in the streets; 
  
 
(B)  Secure safety from fire, panic and other dangers; 
  
 
(C)  Promote health and general welfare; 
  
 
(D)  Provide adequate light and air; 
  
 
(E)  Prevent the overcrowding of land; 
  
 
(F)  Avoid undue concentration of population; and 
  
 
(G)  Facilitate adequate provisions for transportation, water, sewerage, schools, 
parks and other public requirements. 
(ii)  With reasonable consideration, among other things, of the character of the district 
and its peculiar suitability for particular uses; 
  
(iii)  With a view to conserving the value of buildings and encouraging the most 
appropriate use of land throughout the city or town; and 
(iv)  With consideration given to the historic integrity of certain neighborhoods or districts 
and a view to preserving, rehabilitating and maintaining historic properties and encouraging 
compatible uses within the neighborhoods or districts, but no regulation made to carry out the 
purposes of this paragraph is valid to the extent it constitutes an unconstitutional taking without 
compensation. 
 

PLTA-23-2  
 
 
October 16, 2023 
 
Assessment: This amendment proposes modifying the Unified Development Code to modify 
sections of code in accordance with the general welfare and character of the community. The 
regulations and the modifications to the regulations are allowed by state statute. 
 
3. HEALTH, SAFETY, AND PUBLIC WELFARE:  
“The amendment promotes the general health, safety and public welfare.” 
 
This amendment would not adversely impact the general health, safety and public welfare of 
Cheyenne. While reducing construction costs, the amendment attempts to still assure the 
aesthetic values of the community are met. 
 
 
CONCLUSION:  
 
Staff concludes that this text amendment meets the review criteria of UDC 2.4.1.d. 
 
 
STAFF RECOMMENDATION:  
 
Staff recommends approval of text amendments to the Unified Development Code subsections 6.6.3.b.3, 
6.7.5, and 6.9.3.e as outlined in this staff report and recommended by staff, noting the requests meets 
the review criteria of UDC 2.4.1.d.

1. Details on the public outreach accomplished prior to the Planning Commission mailing

October 16, 2023

# SOURCE_FILE: CHEY-2023-11-07_TotalExtraction_77533875.pdf
Planning and Development Office 
2101 O’Neil Avenue, Suite 202, Cheyenne WY 82001 
(Phone) 307-637-6282   
(Fax) 307-637-6366 
 
 
 
 
MEMO 
To: 
Honorable Mayor Patrick Collins and Members of the City Council 
From: 
Seth Lloyd, Senior Planner 
Subject: Update on the Text Amendment amending various subsections of the Unified Development 
Code (UDC), changing building façade design requirements for multifamily residential 
development   
Date: 
November 21, 2023 
 
The text amendment for multifamily materials had a robust discussion at the Public Services Committee 
on November 7, 2023. Discussion centered around the Staff’s recommendation versus the Planning 
Commission’s recommendation. Other discussion points are also worth addressing.   
 
Background:  
As part of a petition to amend the UDC, a materials for multifamily text amendment was previously 
brought to the Governing Body in 2016. That request centered around replacing materials requirements 
with extra landscaping. It failed by a 5-5 vote on second reading. However, the make-up of the 
Governing Body, Staff, and circumstances have changed since that time. The Affordable Housing Task 
Force discussed the cost of materials and the previous failed text amendment, and requested a different 
change to materials code that would hopefully be more palatable to the Governing Body and 
community (Affordable Housing Task Force Recommendation #6: “Along with the creation of the 
Principal Materials category, we recommend there be a requirement to have at least two different 
principal materials (and/or possibly color variations) for multi-family developments.”). The amendment 
created by Staff represented this balanced approach. 
 
Planning Commission: 
The Planning Commission heard this item on October 16, 2023, and had a long discussion. Public 
comment was largely just from a local architect, and the Planning Commission was sympathetic to the 
architect’s statements. The Planning Commission divided the question into 3 sections. One section was 
postponed (the windows or opening portion – which is not currently in front of the Governing Body). 
With the second portion, the Planning Commission removed the materials and color minimum 
percentages for buildings. With the third section, the Planning Commission just forwarded Staff’s 
recommendation on carports – with the minimum percentage for colors in for carports (which, based on 
the discussion in the Planning Commission meeting, could have been an oversight). At least one Planning 
Commission member would have preferred to postpone all items for more information, and therefore 
voted against the recommendation. It is unclear if that Planning Commissioner was leaning for or against 
the recommendation the Planning Commission ended up making. 
 
 
 
 
  
  
  
  
  
  
  
  Planning and Development Department 
2101 O’Neil Avenue, Suite 202, Cheyenne WY 82001 
(Phone) 307-637-6282 (Fax) 307-637-6366 

Proposed Ordinance: 
It is City Staff’s responsibility to bring to the Governing Body the recommendation as approved by the 
Planning Commission. Therefore, the Ordinance in the packet represents the Planning Commission 
recommendation to eliminate materials entirely and just promote color variation. It does not contain 
the windows portion of Staff’s original recommendation. The Public Services Committee wanted more 
information on Staff’s proposal. Attached is a comparison document, comparing the current code, the 
Planning Commission recommendation, and Staff’s original proposal. A general outline of that document 
is as follows (excluding the windows portion postponed by the Planning Commission): 
- 
6.6.3.b.3 Materials for multi-dwelling buildings (Applicable to apartments in the MR and HR) 
o Current code requires all buildings to have 30% brick or stone as ‘primary materials’. As 
carports are a ‘building’ for the purposes of height and setback requirements, materials 
have been required on carports. 
o Planning Commission recommended removal of all materials requirements, but with the 
retention of color variation without a color percentage (except that carports have a 
maximum of 70% of a color – a possible oversight). 
o Staff recommended essentially combining primary materials and secondary materials 
into one and requiring 2 of any of them with color variation. The recommendation to 
provide color and material variation is to retain some building interest. 
- 
6.7.5.c Building Materials (Applicable to mixed-use buildings and “Urban Lofts” [an alternate to 
apartments] in NB, MUR, and MUB zones). 
o Current code requires all buildings to have 30% brick or stone as ‘primary materials’. As 
carports are a ‘building’ for the purposes of height and setback requirements, materials 
have been required on carports. 
o Planning Commission recommended removal of all materials requirements for 
multifamily and mixed-use buildings, but with the retention of color variation without a 
color percentage (except that carports have a maximum of 70% of a color – a possible 
oversight). 
o Staff recommended essentially allowing majority residential structures to utilize 
secondary materials as a primary material with color variation. The recommendation to 
provide color and material variation is to retain some building interest. The proposal 
was targeted to multifamily and left commercial buildings unchanged. 
- 
6.9.4.e Building Materials (Applicable to mixed-use buildings and apartments in CB zoning). The 
current code, the Planning Commission recommendation, and Staff’s recommendation on this 
item are essentially the same as for 6.7.5.c (above). 
 
The Public Services Committee requested Staff bring forward a substitute that reflected Staff’s original 
recommendation – that is attached as “Substitute Option A”. Based on conversations at that meeting, 
Staff has also prepared a “Substitute Option B” which removes requirements for carports. 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
   

Outreach: 
As part of this project, Staff sent the proposed amendments directly to developers, builders, and 
architects via email on October 4, 2023 inviting discussion and comments. Staff has also conducted 
direct outreach to members of the community in an effort to gauge the community’s opinions on the 
item and whether or not Staff was on an acceptable track. The outreach to the general community done 
by Staff is not intended to be a direct democracy vote of the community, but was an effort to engage 
people who would not otherwise go out of their way to attend open houses or City Council meetings.  
The results of the community outreach to date: 
- 
About 1/3 said they ‘didn’t care’ (this did not include people who said they had no time or who 
did not engage) 
- 
Of the 2/3 who responded and said they cared: 
o B was preferred most frequently, with E and A also receiving favorable reviews. 
o F and C were disliked, with G also receiving mostly negative responses. 
o D often elicited strong emotion (either favorable or unfavorable). 
  
Discussion by Local Architects: 
As part of the process, local architects have been very vocal about the code for materials being a 
hindrance to good design and have suggested a UDC overhaul, with complete elimination of materials 
requirements. They feel materials code regulations hinder creativity. City Staff is not opposed to looking 
at façade elements of the UDC in a robust manner and do not take issue with the general substance of 
the architects’ comments to review the UDC for appropriate regulatory language. The UDC annual 
review occurs every year and every member of the community can submit a form online at any time to 
request Staff review changes to portions of the UDC. The carports portion of this amendment was due 
to just such a submittal. The architect community can and should bring this item forward through that 
process.  
 
The Ordinance in front of Council is intended to be the materials portion of a larger suite of multifamily-
based text amendments, not the multifamily portion of a suite of façade-based text amendments. The 
City can re-amend the materials sections proposed for modification with this amendment with a more 
robust façade amendment at a later date. 
 
Although the amendment at hand is not intended to be a robust revision of all façade requirements in 
code, Staff believes a few of the items brought up by the architects deserve some clarification: 
- 
PUDs exist with different materials requirements than typical code, and any developer can 
propose a PUD to change façade requirements (to be more restrictive or less restrictive). A PUD 
would receive the review of the Governing Body. 
- 
The CBD zone has no materials requirements, and the UU overlay in the West Edge can use 
atypical design. 
- 
The UDC materials requirements do not apply to single family or duplex development. Any such 
homes with brick or stone waistcoats are done at option of the builder or are in a PUD.

4. Substitute Ordinance Dated 11/16/2023: Staff’s original recommendation less the carport colors 
requirement

(a) Primary Materials. All buildings shall

least 30% of the building facades. 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
(b) Secondary Materials. Buildings may 
have a secondary material covering up 
to 70% of the façades. 
(c) Accent Materials. Buildings shall have 
at least one, and may have up to two 
accent or trim materials covering  
between 5% and 10% of the building 
facades.

Mayor Collins and Cheyenne City Council 
2101 O’Neil Ave. 
Cheyenne, WY  82001 
 
Recommendation Number Six, Materials Variation Recommendation 
 
Dear Mayor Collins and Members of the City Council, 
 
We the members of the Affordable Housing Task Force recommend the Mayor’s Office and City 
Council/City Staff merge the Primary and Secondary Building Materials category into one Principal 
Materials category. The requirement of Brick or Stone as a primary building material for multi-family 
housing is solely for aesthetic purposes and therefore adds unnecessary costs to the development. Along 
with the creation of the Principal Materials category, we recommend there be a requirement to have at 
least two different principal materials (and/or possibly color variations) for multi-family developments. 
An example of creating an aesthetically pleasing multifamily development without utilizing brick or stone, 
by utilizing other variations can be found in the Granite Springs Apartments located at 908 Jefferson Road 
(Attachment 1). These recommendations would still allow for aesthetically pleasing developments 
without adding unnecessary costs.  
 
Respectfully, 
 
 
Brenda L. Birkle, Chair 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 

Attachment 1

ORDINANCE NO. ________ 
 
ENTITLED: "AN ORDINANCE AMENDING SUBSECTIONS 6.6.3.b.3, 6.7.5.c, AND 
6.9.3.e OF THE UNIFIED DEVELOPMENT CODE (UDC), CHANGING 
BUILDING FAÇADE DESIGN REQUIREMENTS FOR MULTIFAMILY 
RESIDENTIAL DEVELOPMENT." 
 
 
BE IT ORDAINED BY THE GOVERNING BODY OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE, 
WYOMING: 
 
Section 1.  That the Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne finds that the text amendment 
that is the subject of this ordinance meets the criteria specified in Section 2.4.1.d of the Unified 
Development Code. 
 
Section 2.  That on October 16, 2023, the Planning Commission conducted a public 
hearing, reviewed the proposed text amendment, and recommended approval of said text 
amendment. 
 
Section 3.  That Subsection 6.6.3.b.3 Materials for Multi-dwelling buildings, of Subsection 
6.6.3.b Building Design, of Subsection 6.6.3 General Residential Design, of Section 6.6 
Residential Design Standards, of Article 6 Design Regulations, of the Unified Development Code 
of the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, is hereby amended as set out in this section. 
 
3. Materials for Multi-dwelling buildings. All attached dwelling and multi-dwelling 
building types shall be constructed according to the following material standards. 
Percentage requirements for materials exclude windows or other openings. Carports, 
stand-alone canopies, or other structures which only consist of vertical support posts 
in the area between 2’ and 8’ above grade and are less than 2 stories in height shall be 
exempt from the materials requirements of this section. 
(a) Primary Materials. All buildings shall have one not less than two primary 
materials covering at least 30%90% of the building facades. No one primary 
material may cover more than 70% of the facades. No one color shall cover more 
than 70% of the facades (colors shall be distinctly different, if there is a debate 
over whether the proposed colors are distinctly different, an applicant may appeal 
the Director’s determination to the Board of Adjustment). 
(b) Secondary Materials. Buildings may have a secondary material covering up to 
70% of the façades. 
(c)(b) 
Accent Materials. Buildings shall have at least one, and may have up to 
two accent or trim materials covering between 5% and 10% of the building 
facades. The accent shall be a separate material from the primary materials 
utilized on the building. 
(d)(c) 
Permitted Materials. Exterior materials shall be permitted in accordance 
with the Table 6-18.

(f)(e) 
Equal or Better Simulated Materials. Where “equal or better” simulated 
materials are permitted, they shall only be permitted by the Director as an 
Administrative Adjustment according to procedures and criteria in Article 2. The 
Director shall use the following additional criteria: 
(1) The proposed material has an identical physical appearance as the material it 
intended to simulate, including color and texture, as perceived from any 
public spaces surrounding the property; 
(2) The material presents benefits in addition to cost savings, such as reduced 
maintenance, or enhanced durability and performance; 
(3) The material has a demonstrated track record of successful application and use 
that will result in equally long-lasting and sustainable appeal of the 
neighborhood as that of the permitted materials; and 
(4) Use of equal or better materials may be conditioned upon compliance with all 
application and manufacturer specifications.   
 
Section 4.  That Subsection 6.7.5.c Building Materials, of Subsection 6.7.5 Building 
Design, of Section 6.7 Small Scale Commercial and Mixed-use Design Standards,  of Article 6 
Design Regulations, of the Unified Development Code of the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, is 
hereby amended as set out in this section. 
 
c. Building Materials. All buildings and accessory buildings over 200 square feet shall be 
constructed according to the following material standards. Percentage requirements for 
materials exclude windows or other openings. Carports, stand-alone canopies, or other 

structures which only consist of vertical support posts in the area between 2’ and 8’ 
above grade and are less than 2 stories in height shall be exempt from the materials 
requirements of this section. 
1. Primary Materials. All buildings shall have one primary material covering at least 
30% of the building facades. Buildings which are more than 50% residential dwelling 
units (by square footage) may use any of the Secondary Materials as a primary 
material provided: 
(a) No less than 2 primary/secondary materials are used; 
(b) No one primary/secondary material covers more than 70% of the facades; and 
(c)  No one color covers more than 70% of the facades (colors shall be distinctly 
different, not shades of the same color – if there is a debate over whether the 
proposed colors are distinctly different, an applicant may appeal the Director’s 
determination to the Board of Adjustment).

the façades.

shall be a separate material from the primary materials  utilized on the building.

ORDINANCE NO. ________ 
 
ENTITLED: "AN ORDINANCE AMENDING THE OFFICIAL ZONING MAP OF THE 
CITY OF CHEYENNE CHANGING THE ZONING CLASSIFICATION 
FROM CB COMMUNITY BUSINESS
AND HR HIGH-DENSITY 
RESIDENTIAL TO MUB MIXED-USE BUSINESS FOR LAND LOCATED 
NORTHWEST 
OF 
THE 
INTERSECTION 
OF 
DELL 
RANGE 
BOULEVARD AND WHITNEY ROAD."
BE IT ORDAINED BY THE GOVERNING BODY OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE, 
WYOMING:
 
Section 1. That the owner(s) of the property described herein have applied for a zoning 
map amendment ("zone change") for said property in accordance with Section 2.2.1, Zoning Map 
Amendment, of the Cheyenne Unified Development Code ("UDC").
Section 2. That on November 6, 2023, the City of Cheyenne Planning Commission 
conducted a public hearing, reviewed the proposed zone change that is the subject of this 
ordinance, and recommended approval of said zone change. 
Section 3. That the Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne finds that the zone change 
that is the subject of this ordinance meets the criteria specified in Section 2.2.1.d of the UDC.
Section 4.  That, in accordance with Section 2.2.1, Zoning Map Amendment, Section 5.1.2,
Zoning Districts Established, and Section 5.1.3, Official Zoning Map, of the UDC, the 
aforementioned application is hereby approved, and the zoning classification of the real property 
described as Lots 1 and 2, Block 8, Whitney Ranch, 4th Filing is hereby changed to MUB Mixed-
Use Business. 
Section 5. That the City Engineer is hereby authorized and directed to alter and amend the 
Official Zoning Map as described in Section 5.1.3 of the UDC to comply with the zone change as 
described in Section 4 above when this ordinance takes effect in accordance with Section 7 below.
Section 6. Sections, paragraphs, sentences, clauses and phrases of this ordinance are 
severable. If any section, paragraph, sentence, clause, or phrase of this ordinance is declared 
unconstitutional, such unconstitutionality shall not affect any of the remaining sections, 
paragraphs, sentences, clauses, and phrases. 
Section 7. That this ordinance shall be in full force and effect upon its approval and 
publication.

CITY OF CHEYENNE PLANNING COMMISSION
NOVEMBER 6, 2023
STAFF REPORT
FILE:
PUDC-23-149: Block 8, Whitney Ranch 4th Filing
Zoning Map 
Amendment (zone change)
REQUEST:
A request to rezone approximately 11.6 acres of land from 
CB Community Business and HR High Density Residential to MUB Mixed-
Use Business 
LOCATION:
Lots 1 and 2, Block 8, Whitney Ranch 4th Filing
APPLICANT:
Brad Emmons, AVI, PC
AGENT:
Brad Emmons, AVI, PC
OWNER:
Whitney Ranch Third and Fourth Filing LLC
ZONING:
CB Community Business and HR High Density Residential
FUTURE LAND USE:
Mixed-Use Commercial and Urban Residential
PURPOSE:
To allow for multifamily residential development and possible commercial 
development in a mixed-use format
PREPARED BY:
Seth Lloyd, Senior Planner
Planning and Development Department
(Phone) 307-637-6282
(Fax) 307-637-6366

PUDC-23-149
November 6, 2023
RECOMMENDED MOTION: 
 
Move to approve the rezoning of Lots 1 and 2, Block 8, Whitney Ranch 4th Filing to MUB Mixed-Use 
Business as shown in the attached zone change map, noting that the project meets the review criteria 
for approval. 
 
APPLICABLE CITY CODE SECTION(S) AND PLANS: 
 
 
UDC 2.2.1 Zoning Map Amendment 
 
UDC Article 5 
 
PlanCheyenne 
 
BACKGROUND: 
 
This zone change application proposes changing the zoning from CB Community Business and  
HR High-density Residential to MUB Mixed-Use Business for 11.6 acres of vacant land. The properties are 
anticipated to have a multifamily housing and possible commercial uses in a mixed-use format. The 
properties collectively were anticipated to develop with this mix of uses, just with a more defined separation. 
The MUB zone on the entire block gives more flexibility to the location of these uses. 
 
SURROUNDING LAND USE AND ZONING: 
 
 
ANALYSIS OF APPLICATION CONFORMANCE WITH REVIEW CRITERIA: 
 
Should the Planning Commission wish to recommend approval of the Zoning Map Amendment, the 
Commission must make the following findings required in UDC 2.2.1.d. A detailed analysis is below: 
 
1) The application is in accordance with the development policies and physical patterns 
identified in the Comprehensive Plan.  (UDC 2.2.1.d.1) 
 
PlanCheyenne encourages growth within the Urban Service Boundary and the maximization of 
infrastructure. This property is within the Urban Service Boundary, in an area with planned 
infrastructure improvements. Policies in PlanCheyenne encourage development with a balance 
of uses, including higher-density housing along major streets. 
 
Future Land Use Designation  
Zoning District 
Land Use 
Subject 
Site 
Mixed-Use Commercial 
and Urban Residential 
CB Community Business 
and HR High-density 
Residential
Vacant

Urban Residential

Residential and MUB Mixed-
Use Business

and Urban Residential

Urban Residential 
MUB and MR  
Vacant (recently platted) 

PUDC-23-149
November 6, 2023
The Future Land Use Designation for this property is Mixed-Use Commercial and Urban
Residential and the surrounding area is Mixed-Use Commercial and Urban Residential. The area 
designated Mixed-use Commercial is diagonal and not based on any plats, as the designation 
pre-dates the plats. The Mixed-use Commercial designation should therefore be seen as having 
a flexible boundary with the Urban Residential areas to the north and west. The optimal City 
zoning for the future land use designation of Mixed-use Commercial is MUB Mixed-use Business, 
which is the proposed zone of this application (the existing CB zone was an acceptable 
alternative).  
 
The proposed zone change complies with review criterion 1. 
 
2) The application is compatible with nearby property, specifically considering existing zoning, 
the Comprehensive Plan, and any other official plans and policies created under the guidance 
of that plan for these areas; (UDC 2.2.1.d.2) 
 
Nearby properties to the north and west are zoned MUB (the zone that is proposed with this 
zoning map amendment). Property to the northwest is zoned MR. The MR zoned area was 
already adjacent to MUB zoning and is currently undeveloped. Property to the east is zoned CB, 
and MUB is considered a less intense zone, compatible with the commercial nature of that 
zoning. Property to the south is zoned AR in the County. However, this land is adjacent to major 
streets and the future land use designations in the area clearly anticipate more intense uses. 
The MUB zone is considered a less intense zone than the existing CB zone. 
 
The area proposed to be zoned MUB is adjacent to major roadways and is generally compatible 
with anticipated development along major transportation corridors. 
 
The proposed zone change for the subject property complies with review criterion 2. 
   
3) The suitability of the subject property for development according to the standards and uses of 
the existing zoning district, compared to the suitability of the subject property for 
development according to the standards and uses of the proposed zoning district (UDC 
2.2.1.d.3) 
  
The current CB zone allows a variety of commercial uses but prohibits single-unit residential 
uses and requires multifamily to go through a conditional use process. The CB zone requires 
large setbacks from streets. The proposed MUB zone allows a variety of commercial uses, 
residential uses, and mixed uses. The MUB zone also allows buildings to be built to the street. 
The MUB zone typically has a higher design standard than the CB zone. 
 
The proposed zone change complies with review criterion 3. 
 
4) The ability of the subject property and surrounding vicinity to be developed according to the 
Subdivision Standards in Article 4 considering the proposed zoning (UDC 2.2.1.d.4) 
 
The existing block is larger than expected for the urban-style of development anticipated for 
MUB zoning. However, the block qualifies for exceptions due to the proximity to a major street 
intersection. 
 
The proposed zone change for the subject property complies with review criterion 4. 
 

PUDC-23-149
November 6, 2023
5)
The ability of the application to support the Intent and Applicability statements of the 
proposed zoning district and achieve the Design Objectives of any specific standards. (UDC 
2.2.1.d.5) 
 
The MUB zone is intended for a diverse mix of residential, retail, service, employment and civic 
uses integrated into a walkable, compact development pattern. The MUB District is applicable to 
areas where more intense development and diverse mix of uses is appropriate with a high level 
of pedestrian and civic amenities. 
 
The proposed development pattern includes mixed-use and residential zones to the north and 
west in a connected block pattern, allowing nearby residents in that area to walk between the 
proposed development areas. 
 
The proposed zone change for the subject property complies with review criterion 5. 
    
6) The ability of all potential future development to meet all applicable standards for 
development in the proposed zoning district, including other development options if any 
specific development proposed in association with the application is not executed after 
approval. (UDC 2.2.1.d.6) 
 
The existing property meets the standards of the MUB zone. The MUB zone allows a building to 
be brought closer to the street, while a new building on this property in the CB zone would 
 Specifically proposed is a multifamily development with 
a potential mixed-use element. Should this project not be executed, the MUB zone allows a 
variety of uses. 
 
The proposed zone change complies with review criterion 6. 
 
7) The review and recommendations of other reviewing bodies. (UDC 2.2.1.d.7) 
 
No reviewing agencies provided recommendations regarding the proposed zone change. 
 
The proposed zone change for the subject property complies with review criterion 7. 
 
8) Whether the application will harm the public health, safety, or general welfare. (UDC 
2.2.1.d.8) 
 
City staff is satisfied that this proposed zone change would not adversely affect the public 
health, safety, or general welfare. 
 
The proposed zone change for the subject property complies with review criterion 8. 
 
CONFORMANCE WITH APPLICABLE PLANS: 
 
The project is located in an area covered by the following plans: 
 
PlanCheyenne 
Conformance with PlanCheyenne is covered by review criterion 1.     
 
 
 

PUDC-23-149
November 6, 2023
STAFF REVIEW: 
 
Through the review process the no comments were received. 
 
CONCLUSION: 
 
Staff is satisfied that the application meets the review criteria set forth in UDC 2.2.1.(d).  The MUB 
Mixed-Use Business is appropriate based on the future land use map, surrounding zoning, and 
surrounding development patterns. 
 
ALTERNATIVES: 
 
1. Recommend approval of the zoning amendment as submitted by the applicant (Staff 
Recommendation).  
2. Recommend approval of the zoning amendment subject to conditions or modifications.  
3. Recommend denial of the zoning amendment based on findings of denial. The Planning 
Commission must conclude that the project does not meet all the review criteria. As part of the 
motion, findings for denial must be stated. 
4. Postpone the zoning amendment until issues identified during the meeting can be resolved. 
 
General Information Regarding Alternatives: 
 
If 
Commission shall cite specific reasons (based on the review criteria) for approval or denial. 
 
STAFF RECOMMENDATION: 
 
Staff recommends approval of the rezoning of the property from CB and HR to MUB

and became effective  April 11, 2023)

# SOURCE_FILE: CHEY-2023-11-07_TotalExtraction_70917336.pdf
Planning and Development Office 
2101 O’Neil Avenue, Suite 202, Cheyenne WY 82001 
(Phone) 307-637-6282   
(Fax) 307-637-6366 
 
 
 
 
MEMO 
To: 
Honorable Mayor Patrick Collins and Members of the City Council 
From: 
Charles W. Bloom, AICP 
Subject: County Pocket Annexation #2   
Date: 
October 16, 2023 
 
Background:  
 
The item for consideration is a resolution to allow the City to proceed with a City-initiated annexation. 
Annexation of County pockets is a 2022 City Council priority.  A “County pocket” is an area of 
unincorporated land 100% surrounded by the City of Cheyenne.  A related “Assigned Zoning” 
pursuant to Unified Development Code (UDC) Section 2.2.8 ordinance is also on the agenda.    
 
The proposed Governing Body review schedule is as follows: 
• 
Introduction and referral: November 13, 2023 
• 
Public Services Committee: November 21, 2023 
• 
Second Reading: November 27, 2023 
• 
Public Services Committee: December 5, 2023 
• 
Third and Final Reading: December 11, 2023 
 
This annexation is comprised of 51 properties 100% surrounded by the City (See attached map). Of 
the 50 parcels, 26 are developed with residential uses, one is developed with a commercial use, and 
others are undeveloped or incorporated into adjacent parcels under similar ownership. The City has 
maintained contact with the County Planning Office to coordinate any pending permits or 
development reviews.  Currently there are no active permits, development specific to the properties 
proposed for annexation, or identified zoning violations.   
 
On September 20, 2023, City staff mailed a courtesy notice by first class mail to all landowners 
included in this annexation area inviting them to a “Question and Answer” on Tuesday, October 3, 
2023.  A media release was also released on September 22, 2023, identifying properties included in 
this Count pocket annexation.  Seventeen people attended this meeting. Discussion, comments, and 
questions primarily surrounded the annexation component, but the topics of zoning, nonconforming 
uses, livestock, and animals did occur. During the meeting staff provided information regarding 
assigned zoning and learned that some non-residential accessory uses and home-based businesses 
existed within the area including an animal boarding, auction house, warehousing, and shipping and 
receiving operations were occurring. 
 
  
  
  
  
  
  
  
  Planning and Development Department 
2101 O’Neil Avenue, Suite 202, Cheyenne WY 82001 
(Phone) 307-637-6282 (Fax) 307-637-6366 

The existing use of any property annexed, including any livestock use, will be allowed to continue as is 
as a nonconforming use.  Nonconforming uses may continue until they are abandoned for a period of 
12-months. Residents within pockets have been encouraged to complete a Land Use Documentation 
form detailing the existing use of the land for documentation purposes.  At the time of drafting of this 
memo, one property owner within the area has completed a land use documentation form which is 
attached.  Providing a completed form is not required nor does it prohibit a property owner from 
making future claims that a use was pre-exiting.  The form is simply a tool to assist future City staff 
members to determine if a use was in existence at the time of annexation.   
 
City-initiated annexations do not require an owner’s signature. To initiate an annexation, the City must 
adopt a resolution certifying that the annexation complies with Wyoming State Statute § 15-1-402. After 
the City adopts this resolution, the annexation follows the typical schedule, including a public hearing and 
the approval of an Ordinance which annexes the land. The Governing Body adopted Resolution No. 6357 
initiating this annexation on October 9, 2023. 
 
As with any annexation, City services will be extended to service the land contained in the annexation. City 
utilities are already located in the vicinity. Police, fire, and sanitation services will be provided.  As several 
parcels of land contained in this annexation area are developed, City sanitation service will need to be 
established and landowners have been notified of this required change.  Infrastructure improvements 
required related to the annexation are minimal. Future development such as subdivision or new 
development will require on- and off-site infrastructure improvements. Existing infrastructure will remain 
as is upon annexation.  All roads adjacent to annexed parcels are within City limits and maintained by the 
City.  
 
The areas proposed for annexation are adjacent to land under City jurisdiction. Development in this area is 
a natural extension of the City. 100 % of the perimeter of the annexation area is contiguous to City limits.   
 
Due to meeting the review criteria found in Wyoming Statute § 15-1-402 and the annexation conforming 
with the 2022 City Council goal to annex County pockets, staff recommends approval of this annexation. 
 
Staff Recommendation: 
 
Staff recommends the Governing Body approve the Annexation Ordinance.  
 
Attachments: 
1. Completed Land Use Documentation form (1) 
2. Proposed Ordinance (includes Annexation Map as Attachment A)

SAID PARCELS CONTAIN 10.32 ACRES MORE OR LESS; 
 
(c) That the annexation of said area is for the protection of health, safety, and welfare 
of the persons residing in the area and the City of Cheyenne; 
(d) That the urban development of the area sought to be annexed will constitute a 
natural, geographical, economical and social part of the City of Cheyenne; 
(e) That the area is a logical and feasible addition to the City and that the extension of 
basic services such as water and sewer systems, police and fire protection and other 
services customarily available in the City of Cheyenne can, within reason, be 
furnished; 
(f) That the area is considered contiguous to the City of Cheyenne according to the 
provisions of Wyo. Stat. § 15-1-402 (b) and is currently zoned X Exempt by the 

Laramie County Commissioners; and 
(g) That the City of Cheyenne does not own or operate its own electrical utility, and its 
governing body is prepared to designate a utility to serve the annexed area pursuant 
to Wyo. Stat. § 15-1-410, and to authorize the designated utility to serve the entire 
annexed area. 
 
Section 2.  That all of the real property described in Section 1(b) of this Ordinance is hereby 
annexed to the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, and shall become a part thereof.  
 
Section 3.  That pursuant to Wyoming Statute § 15-1-405, the Governing Body held a 
public hearing on November 13, 2023, on the proposed and annexation and found that the 
annexation complied Wyo. Stat. § 15-1-402.   
 
Section 4.  That the map of the area to be annexed is shown in Exhibit A, which is attached 
hereto and incorporated herein, satisfies the requirements of Wyo. Stat. § 15-1-406 and shall be 
recorded with the county clerk in conjunction with this ordinance. 
 
Section 5.  That the City Clerk is directed to file an executed copy of this annexation 
ordinance with the County Clerk and Ex-Officio Register of Deeds for Laramie County, Wyoming, 
and shall also notify the City Engineer's Office to add said area to the official City Map of the City 
of Cheyenne, Wyoming. 
 
Section 6.  Sections, paragraphs, sentences, clauses and phrases of this ordinance are 
severable. If any phrase, clause, sentence, paragraph or section of this ordinance is declared illegal 
or unconstitutional, such illegality or unconstitutionality shall not affect any of the remaining 
phrases, clauses, sentences, paragraphs and sections.  
 
Section 7.  That this ordinance shall be in full force and effect upon its publication.

MEMO 
To: 
Honorable Mayor Patrick Collins and Members of the City Council 
From: 
Charles W. Bloom, AICP 
Subject: PUDC-23-163 Assigned Zoning County Pocket #2 Annexation   
Date: 
November 13, 2023 
 
Background:  
The item for consideration is a Zone Change. This is approved by ordinance. This zoning map amendment 
is City-initiated in conjunction with a City-initiated annexation under Wyo. Stat. § 15-1-404. Because 
this is a City-initiated annexation, the City must establish City Zone Districts. The area to be annexed 
and zoned is comprised of 51 parcels.  Of those parcels, 26 appear to be primarily developed with 
residential uses and one solely as a business use.  
 
The proposed Governing Body review schedule is as follows: 
• 
Introduction and referral: November 13, 2023 
• 
Public Services Committee: November 21, 2023 
• 
Second Reading: November 27, 2023 
• 
Public Services Committee: December 5, 2023 
• 
Third and Final Reading: December 11, 2023 
 
On July 10, 2023, the Governing Body approved Ordinance No. 4516 creating UDC Section 2.2.8, 
Assigned Zoning, to provide review standards and procedures for zoning districts established on land 
or property currently without a City zone assignment. Establishment of these zone districts for these 
areas follows the Assigned Zoning process. 
 
Attached to this memo are the Assigned Zoning Chart, Laramie County Land Use Regulations: Chapter 
2, Zone Districts, and Cheyenne Unified Development Code: Use Table 5-1 Zone Districts and Uses 
which detail any differences in land use between the County and City zones.  Staff has highlighted text 
applicable to this zoning action. 
 
The Planning Commission held a public hearing on November 6, 2023, and after hearing public 
comment, recommended approval of the Assigned Zoning as recommended by staff.     
 
Attachments: 
1. November 6, 2023, Planning Commission Staff Report 
2. Assigned Zone Chart 
3. Laramie County Land Use Regulations: Chapter 2, Zone Districts 
4. Cheyenne Unified Development Code: Use Table 5-1 Zone Districts and Uses 
5. Proposed Ordinance
