(Phone) 307-637-6282 (Fax) 307-637-6366 

PUDC-23-163 
 
 
November 6, 2023 
Planning and Development Office 
2101 O’Neil Avenue, Suite 202, Cheyenne WY 82001 
(Phone) 307-637-6282   (Fax) 307-637-6366 
 
 
 
 
CITY OF CHEYENNE PLANNING COMMISSION 
 
NOVEMBER 6, 2023 
 
STAFF REPORT 
 
 
FILE: 
 
PUDC-23-163:  County Pocket #2 Annexation – Establishment of Zoning 
REQUEST: 
City-initiated rezoning of various properties from County A1, County LR, 
County MU, County MR, and County CB to City AG, City LR, City MR, City 
MUR, and City CB concurrent with a City-initiated annexation  
LOCATION: 
51 parcels within nine County pockets generally located north of Dell 
Range Boulevard and west of Powderhouse Road.  
APPLICANT: 
City of Cheyenne 
OWNER: 
Various owners  
ZONING: 
County A1, County LR, County MU, County MR 
FUTURE LAND USE: 
Urban Residential, Mixed Use Commercial, and Parks and Open Space  
PURPOSE: 
To establish a City zoning district concurrent with annexation 
PREPARED BY: 
Charles Bloom, AICP, Planning and Development Director 
 
RECOMMENDED MOTION: 
 
Move to recommend the Governing Body approve the establishment of City zones associated with the 
County Pockets #2 annexation as follows: All County A1 Agricultural and Rural Residential zoned areas 
be zoned AG Agriculture, all County LR Low Density Residential zoned areas be zoned LR Low Density 
Residential, all County MR Medium Density Residential zoned areas be zoned MR Medium Density 
Residential, all County CB Community Business zoned areas be zoned CB Community Business, and all 
County MU Mixed Use zoned areas be zoned MUR Mixed-use Residential as shown in the attached zone 
change map, noting that the project meets the review criteria for approval. 
 
 
 
  
  
  
  
  
  
  
  Planning and Development Department 
2101 O’Neil Avenue, Suite 202, Cheyenne WY 82001 
(Phone) 307-637-6282 
(Fax) 307-637-6366 

PUDC-23-163 
November 6, 2023 
APPLICABLE CITY CODE SECTION(S) AND PLANS: 
•
UDC 2.2.8 Assigned Zoning
•
City Council 2022 Goal: Annexation of County Pockets
BACKGROUND: 
This zoning map amendment is City-initiated in conjunction with a City-initiated annexation under Wyo. Stat. § 
15-1-404.   City zoning districts are established in conjunction with annexation typically to support a proposed 
development.  Because this is a City-initiated annexation, the City must establish City Zone Districts.  The area 
to be annexed and zoned is comprised of 51 parcels.  Of those parcels, 26 appear to be primarily developed 
with residential uses and one solely as a business use. 
On July 10, 2023, the Governing Body approved Ordinance No. 4516 creating UDC Section 2.2.8, Assigned 
Zoning, to provide review standards and procedures for zoning districts established on land or property 
currently without a City zone assignment. Concurrent with the adoption of the Assigned Zoning process, staff 
created a policy document indicating what the corollary city zone would be to the underlying county 
zone.  
The procedures for assigned zoning are similar to that of a zoning map amendment. Posted notice is 
required and notice is mailed to property owners within the area to be zoned and within 300’ of those 
areas. The primary difference with Assigned Zoning is that only one review criteria exists and that 
criteria is detailed in the analysis section below.    
Question-and-Answer Session: 
A question-and-answer session was held on October 8, 2023. Only property owners within the 
annexation area were invited. Seventeen people attended. Discussion, comments, and questions 
primarily surrounded the annexation component, but the topics of zoning, nonconforming uses, 
livestock, and animals did occur. During the meeting staff provided information regarding assigned 
zoning and learned that some non-residential accessory uses and home-based businesses existed within 
the area including an animal boarding, auction house, warehousing, and shipping and receiving 
operations were occurring.   
Nonconforming Uses: 
Any non-conforming uses will be subject to the nonconforming use provisions of the Unified Development 
Code (UDC § 1.3.6). In the event a nonconforming use wishes to change, expand, or intensify approvals may 
be granted by the Board of Adjustment through Conditional Use approval pursuant to UDC § 1.3.6.c.1.  
Furthermore, pursuant to Wyo. Stat. § 15-1-410(a) the City  
“(a) The territory and inhabitants of any annexed area are subject to all the laws, ordinances, 
rules and regulations of the city or town to which they are annexed and are entitled to all the 
rights, privileges and franchises or other services afforded the inhabitants thereof. The services 
shall be provided in accordance with the timetable provided pursuant to W.S. 15-1-402(c)(iii). 
Notwithstanding any other provision of law, no law, ordinance, rule or regulation of a 
municipality annexing property under W.S. 15-1-404 shall restrict the continuous use of the 
property by a current or subsequent owner of an interest in the property, if the use was existing 

PUDC-23-163 
 
 
November 6, 2023 
at any time within the year prior to the date of annexation and was lawful at the time the 
property was annexed. For purposes of this subsection, a use which has been discontinued for 
any one (1) year period after the date of annexation shall not be considered continuous and 
shall not thereafter be reestablished unless in conformance with current law, ordinance, rule or 
regulation.” 
Staff reached out to the County Planning and Development Department, and they have not expressed 
concern with any land uses in the areas to be zoned.  The City will accept these areas “as-is” and the 
existing uses will be grandfathered in.  Property owners may voluntarily complete the Land Use 
Documentation Form on the City’s annexation website to provide information regarding the use of their 
land.  To date, staff has received several of these forms.      
ANALYSIS OF APPLICATION CONFORMANCE WITH REVIEW CRITERIA: 
 
Should the Planning Commission wish to recommend approval of the Zoning Map Amendment, the 
Commission must make the following findings required in UDC 2.2.1.d. A detailed analysis is below: 
 
1) The assigned zone is the most analogous City zone to the current zone when considering 
allowed uses. Zoning for standard County zones shall be assigned based on the policy 
document, while zones not listed on the policy document and terminated City zones shall be 
assigned a City zone on a case-by-case basis. (UDC 2.2.8.d.1) 
 
The Assigned Zoning policy document is shown below.  
 
Establishment of the City zones matches the policy document; therefore, the item complies with 
review criterion 1. 
 
 
 

PUDC-23-163 
 
 
November 6, 2023 
STAFF REVIEW: 
 
Through the review process, there were no comments.  
 
CONCLUSION: 
 
Staff is satisfied that the application meets the review criteria set forth in UDC 2.2.8.(d). 
 
ALTERNATIVES: 
 
1. Recommend approval of the Assigned Zoning as submitted by the applicant (Staff 
Recommendation).  
2. Recommend approval of the Assigned Zoning subject to conditions or modifications.  
3. Recommend denial of the Assigned Zoning based on findings of denial. The Planning 
Commission must conclude that the project does not meet all the review criteria. As part of the 
motion, findings for denial must be stated. 
4. Postpone the Assigned Zoning until issues identified during the meeting can be resolved. 
 
General Information Regarding Alternatives: 
 
If the Commission acts contrary to staff’s recommendation or recommends conditions of approval, the 
Commission shall cite specific reasons (based on the review criteria) for their recommendation. 
 
STAFF RECOMMENDATION: 
 
Staff recommends approval approve the establishment of City zones associated with the County Pockets 
#2 annexation as follows: All County A1 Agricultural and Rural Residential zoned areas be zoned AG 
Agriculture, all County LR Low Density Residential zoned areas be zoned LR Low Density Residential, all 
County MR Medium Density Residential zoned areas be zoned MR Medium Density Residential, all 
County CB Community Business zoned areas be zoned CB Community Business, and all County MU 
Mixed Use zoned areas be zoned MUR Mixed-use Residential as shown in the attached zone change 
map.

The Laramie County Land Use Regulations 
Amended October 4, 2022

CHAPTER 2 ZONE DISTRICTS 
4-2-100 
DISTRICT AR - AGRICULTURAL RESIDENTIAL 
Areas primarily used for large lot detached residential development at a density which typifies a 
rural lifestyle, and which may include some agricultural uses, are considered agricultural residential. 
a. 
Uses by Right 
i. 
Accessory structures 
ii. 
Family child care home 
iii. 
Home occupations 
iv. 
Single-family residential 
v. 
Family child care centers 
vi. 
Bed and breakfasts 
vii. Churches, temples or other places of worship 
viii. Primary and secondary schools 
ix. 
Accessory living quarters 
x. 
Small Wind Energy Systems (Small Wind Energy Systems setbacks shall be equal 
to the largest district setbacks or the total height of the system, whichever is 
greater.) 
b. 
Uses Requiring Board Approval 
The following uses may be permitted by the Board: 
i. 
Animal hospitals or clinics 
ii. 
Commercial stables, arenas, kennels, bird farms and show barns 
iii. 
Child care centers - minor and major 
iv. 
Commercial nurseries or landscaping businesses 
v. 
Other uses similar to those permitted in this district 
c. 
Density 
Minimum area for any use in this district is 5 acres for small wastewater system permits, 
computed consistent with the following exceptions: 
i. 
If the property is served by an approved central water distribution system, and/or 
sewer collection and treatment system, the minimum residential use lot or 
residential use tract area in this district may be reduced subject to a review and 
approval from the Wyoming Department of Environmental Quality. The 
computation of lot or tract sizes in this exception shall not include adjacent public 
or private rights-of-way, easements or reservations for roadway purposes. 
ii. 
Lots or tracts platted and recorded with the County Clerk prior to May 5, 1987 shall 
be a minimum of 2.5 acres for any use in this district. 
The Laramie County Land Use Regulations 
Amended October 4, 2022 
Page | 210 
 
 
 
iii. 
Lots or tracts platted and recorded with the County Clerk prior to June 5, 1979 may 
be used as building sites for uses allowed in this district if adequate provisions can 
be made for water and sewer/septic. 
iv. 
Minimum area for water well systems – refer to Section 2-1-103 c. i. for 
subdivisions approved after the adoption of these regulations. 
v. 
Minimum acreage may be averaged with environmental health 
approval but shall not have an average below 5.25 acres per tract 
unless meeting (c)(i) above. 
vi. 
Setbacks 
vii. Accessory structure setbacks shall conform to Section 2-2-118 of this regulation. 
All other setbacks – refer to table in Section 4-1-104. 
viii. Commercial Use Site Plan Requirements 
ix. 
A County-approved site and landscape plan shall be required in accordance with 
Sections 2-2-133 and 2-2-134 of these regulations. Commercial operations may 
cover 85% of the tract. 
4-2-101 
DISTRICT A1 - AGRICULTURAL AND RURAL RESIDENTIAL 
Areas in a natural state or in which the growing of crops, flowers, trees, or pasture, or the 
production of livestock or other farming or ranching activity is practiced, are considered agricultural 
and rural residential. 
a. 
Uses by Right 
i. 
Agriculture and uses incidental to an agricultural operation 
ii. 
Family child care home 
iii. 
Family child care centers 
iv. 
Home occupations 
v. 
Single-family residential 
vi. 
Small Wind Energy Systems (Small Wind Energy Systems setbacks shall be equal 
to the largest district setbacks or the total height of the system, whichever is 
greater.) 
vii. Animal hospitals or clinics 
viii. Bed and breakfasts 
ix. 
Cemeteries 
x. 
Churches, temples or other places of worship 
xi. 
Duplexes 
xii. Primary and secondary Schools 
xiii. Roadside farm stands 
xiv. Accessory living quarters 
 
b. 
Uses Requiring Board Approval 

The Laramie County Land Use Regulations 
Amended October 4, 2022 
Page | 211 
 
 
 
The following uses may be permitted by the Board: 
i. 
Commercial sales of agricultural related products, not including fuel or petroleum 
products. 
ii. 
Work camps. 
iii. 
Any other similar use. 
iv. 
Commercial stables, arenas, kennels, bird farms and show barns. 
v. 
Commercial nurseries and landscaping businesses. 
c. 
Density 
i. 
The minimum lot size for any use in this district is ten (10) acres. For single family 
uses, the density may be averaged within the subdivision/ development, provided 
that no lot or tract shall be less than six (6) acres gross. 
ii. 
The following exceptions are permissible: 
iii. 
Lots or tracts platted and recorded with the County Clerk prior to November 2, 
1999, shall be a minimum of 5 acres. 
iv. 
Lots or tracts platted and recorded with the County Clerk prior to May 5, 1987, may 
be a minimum of 2.5 acres. 
v. 
Lots or tracts platted and recorded with the County Clerk prior to June 5, 1979, 
may be used as building sites if adequate provisions can be made for water and 
sewer/septic. 
d. 
Setbacks 
Accessory structure setbacks shall conform to Section 2-2-118 of this regulation. All 
other setbacks – refer to table in Section 4-1-104. 
e. 
Commercial Use Site Plan Requirements 
A County-approved site and landscape plan shall be required in accordance with 
Sections 2-2-133 and 2-2-134 of these regulations. Commercial operations may cover 
85% of the tract. 
4-2-102 
DISTRICT A2 - AGRICULTURAL 
Areas in a natural state or in which the growing of crops, flowers, trees, or pasture, or the 
production of livestock, or other farming or ranching activity is practiced, are considered agricultural. 
a. 
Uses by Right 
i. 
Agriculture and uses incidental to an agricultural operation 
ii. 
Animal hospitals or clinics 
iii. 
Bed and breakfasts 
iv. 
Cemeteries 
v. 
Family child care home 
vi. 
Family child care centers 
vii. Home occupations 
The Laramie County Land Use Regulations 
Amended October 4, 2022 
Page | 212 
 
 
 
viii. Single-family residential 
ix. 
Small Wind Energy Systems (Small Wind Energy Systems setbacks shall be equal 
to the largest district setbacks or the total height of the system, whichever is 
greater.) 
x. 
Churches, temples or other places of worship 
xi. 
Duplexes 
xii. Child care centers - minor 
xiii. Primary and secondary schools. 
xiv. Commercial stables, arenas, kennels, bird farms and show barns 
xv. Farm stands 
xvi. Accessory living quarters 
b. 
Uses Requiring Board Approval 
The following uses may be permitted by the Board: 
i. 
Agricultural equipment or product sales 
ii. 
Commercial animal processing plants 
iii. 
Commercial feed lots 
iv. 
Golf courses 
v. 
Race tracks 
vi. 
Work camps 
vii. Any other similar use 
viii. Commercial nurseries and landscaping businesses 
c. 
Density 
The minimum lot size for any use in this district is twenty (20) acres. 
d. 
Setbacks 
Accessory structure setbacks shall conform to Section 2-2-118 of this regulation. All 
other setbacks – refer to table in Section 4-1-104. 
e. 
Commercial Use Site Plan Requirements 
A County-approved site and landscape plan shall be required in accordance with 
Sections 2-2-133 and 2-2-134 of these regulations. Commercial operations may cover 
85% of the tract. 
4-2-103 
DISTRICT LR - LOW DENSITY RESIDENTIAL 
Areas which are primarily used for low density, detached residential uses. 
a. 
Uses by Right 
i. 
Family child care home 
ii. 
Family child care center 

The Laramie County Land Use Regulations 
Amended October 4, 2022 
Page | 213 
 
 
iii. 
Home occupations 
iv. 
Single-family residential 
 
v. 
Duplexes and townhouses, when developed on property platted for duplexes and 
townhouses 
vi. 
Churches, temples or other places of worship 
vii. Primary and secondary schools 
viii. Accessory living quarters 
b. 
Uses Requiring Board Approval 
The following uses may be permitted by the Board: 
i. 
Bed and breakfasts 
ii. 
Group dwellings 
iii. 
Manufactured housing parks 
iv. 
Other uses similar to those permitted in this district 
v. 
Duplexes and townhouses, to include those developed by conversion of existing 
structures (to include single-family residential and commercial structures) or by 
splitting a previously platted lot. 
c. 
Minimum Lot Area and Maximum Building Coverage 
i. 
Minimum Lot Area - 
(A) 
Single-family: 9,000 square feet per unit 
(B) 
Duplex or Townhouses: 4,500 square feet per unit 
ii. 
Maximum Building Coverage - 
(A) 
Total building area shall not exceed 40 percent of the total property. 
d. 
Setbacks 
Accessory structure setbacks shall conform to Section 2-2-118 of this regulation. All 
other setbacks – refer to table in Section 4-1-104. 
e. 
Height 
The maximum building height shall be thirty-five (35) feet. 
Parking 
f. 
Parking shall be provided in conformance with the parking requirements of this 
regulation. Parking lots of five or more spaces shall not be permitted in the required 
setback from any street. This does not pertain to parking for single- family or duplex 
units. 
g. 
Commercial Use Site Plan Requirements 
A County-approved site and landscape plan shall be required in accordance with 
Sections 2-2-133 and 2-2-134 of these regulations
The Laramie County Land Use Regulations 
Amended October 4, 2022 
Page | 214 
 
 
 
4-2-104 
DISTRICT MR - MEDIUM DENSITY RESIDENTIAL 
Areas which are to be primarily developed or redeveloped for medium density residential uses. 
a. 
Uses by Right 
i. 
Family child care homes 
ii. 
Home occupations 
iii. 
Single-family residential 
iv. 
Duplexes and townhouses. 
v. 
Family child care center 
vi. 
Offices when the building area does not exceed 3,500 square feet. There shall be 
no outside storage of equipment and supplies related to the offices. 
vii. Residential accessory uses 
viii. Churches, temples or other places of worship 
ix. 
Primary and secondary schools 
x. 
Multi-family residential 
b. 
Uses Requiring Board Approval 
The following uses may be permitted by the Board: 
i. 
Bed and breakfasts 
ii. 
Dormitories 
iii. 
Group dwellings 
iv. 
Hospitals and nursing homes 
v. 
Manufactured housing parks 
vi. 
Offices 
vii. Other uses similar to those permitted in this district 
viii. Assisted living facilities 
ix. 
Accessory living quarters 
c. 
Minimum Property Area 
i. 
Single-family: 6,000 square feet per unit 
ii. 
Townhouse/Duplex: 3,000 square feet per unit 
iii. 
Multi-family: 2,000 square feet per unit

d. 
Maximum Building Coverage 
Total building area shall not exceed 50 percent of the total property for nonresidential 
uses. 
Clustered development and Best Management Practices for storm water management 
and open space design is encouraged. Characteristics such as shared driveways, 
community open space, pocket parks and connectivity to regional trails, including the 
Greater Cheyenne Greenway, are encouraged. 
e. 
Setbacks 
Accessory structure setbacks shall conform to Section 2-2-118 of this regulation. All 
other setbacks – refer to table in Section 4-1-104. 
f. 
Height 
The maximum building height shall be thirty-five (35) feet. 
g. 
Parking 
Parking lots of five or more spaces shall not be permitted in the required setback from 
any street. This does not pertain to parking for single-family or duplex units. 
h. 
Commercial Use Site Plan Requirements 
A County-approved site and landscape plan shall be required in accordance with 
Sections 2-2-133 and 2-2-134 of these regulations. 
4-2-105 
DISTRICT HR - HIGH DENSITY RESIDENTIAL 
Areas primarily to be developed for a variety of attached and detached high density residential 
uses. 
a. 
Uses by Right 
i. 
Single-family residential 
ii. 
Family child care homes 
iii. 
Home occupations 
iv. 
Duplexes and townhouses. 
v. 
Bed and breakfasts 
vi. 
Churches, temples or other places of worship 
vii. Dormitories 
viii. Family child care center 
ix. 
Hospitals and nursing homes 
x. 
Offices when the building does not exceed 3,500 square feet. There shall be no 
outside storage of equipment and supplies related to the offices. 
xi. 
Primary and secondary schools 
xii. Multi-family residential 
The Laramie County Land Use Regulations 
Amended October 4, 2022 
Page | 216 
 
 
 
b. 
Uses Requiring Board Approval 
The following uses may be permitted by the Board: 
i. 
Group dwellings 
ii. 
Child care center - minor and major 
iii. 
Manufactured housing parks 
iv. 
Other uses similar to those permitted in this district 
v. 
Assisted living facilities 
vi. 
Accessory living quarters 
vii. Mixed-use residential development 
c. 
Minimum Property Area 
i. 
Single-family: 5,000 square feet per unit 
ii. 
Townhouse/duplex: 2,000 square feet per unit 
iii. 
Multi-family: 
(A) 
three (3) stories and less: 1,600 square feet per unit 
(B) 
above three (3) stories: 1,000 square feet per unit 
There shall be a minimum usable open space for multi-family buildings of 1,000 square 
feet per building. Board Approval is required if a portion of the open space requirement 
will be inside the building. 
d. 
Maximum Building Coverage 
Total building area shall not exceed 55 percent of the total property area for 
nonresidential uses. Clustered development and Best Management Practices for storm 
water management and open space design are encouraged. 
Characteristics such as shared driveways, community open space, pocket parks and 
connectivity to regional trails, including the Greater Cheyenne Greenway are 
encouraged. 
e. 
Setbacks 
Accessory structure setbacks shall conform to Section 2-2-118 of this regulation. All 
other setbacks – refer to table in Section 4-1-104. 
f. 
Height 
The maximum building height shall be forty-five (45) feet. 
g. 
Parking 
Parking shall be provided in conformance with the parking requirements of this 
regulation. Parking lots of five (5) or more spaces shall not be permitted in the required 
setback from any street. This does not pertain to parking for single- family or duplex 
units. 

The Laramie County Land Use Regulations 
Amended October 4, 2022 
Page | 217 
 
 
 
h. 
Commercial Use Site Plan Requirements 
A County-approved site and landscape plan shall be required in accordance with 
Sections 2-2-133 and 2-2-134 of these regulations. 
4-2-106 
DISTRICT NB – NEIGHBORHOOD BUSINESS 
Neighborhood business areas are to be primarily used for neighborhood commercial and office 
uses that provide goods and services to area residents. 
a. 
Uses by Right 
i. 
The retail sale of goods or the provision of services as long as all sales and 
storage of equipment and supplies are conducted within a building. 
ii. 
Offices 
iii. 
Child care facilities 
iv. 
Churches, temples or other places of worship 
v. 
Mixed-use residential developments 
b. 
Uses Requiring Board Approval 
The following uses may be permitted by the Board: 
i. 
Bars, cocktail lounges, and liquor stores 
ii. 
Drive-up facilities 
iii. 
The retail sale of goods or the provisions of services that require outside sales and 
storage of supplies and equipment 
iv. 
Other uses similar to those permitted in this district 
c. 
Maximum Property Coverage 
Total building and parking area shall not exceed 75 percent of the total property area. 
d. 
Setbacks 
Accessory structure setbacks shall conform to Section 2-2-118 of this regulation. All 
other setbacks – refer to table in Section 4-1-104. 
e. 
Height 
Building height shall not exceed thirty-five (35) feet. 
f. 
Site Design 
All areas, including areas from right-of-way line to property line that are not covered by 
buildings, sidewalks, and parking area shall be landscaped. 
Best Management Practices for storm water management and open space design are 
encouraged. Characteristics such as community open space, pocket parks and 
connectivity to regional trails, including the Greater Cheyenne Greenway, are 
encouraged. 
g. 
Parking 
Parking requirements and standards are in accordance with this regulation. 
The Laramie County Land Use Regulations 
Amended October 4, 2022 
Page | 218 
 
 
 
h. 
Commercial Use Site Plan Requirements 
A County-approved site and landscape plan shall be required in accordance with 
Sections 2-2-133 and 2-2-134 of these regulations. 
Screening is required in conformance with site plan requirements for outdoor storage 
areas of items and equipment which are not for immediate use, sale or lease. Screening 
for outdoor storage areas shall be located behind the setback line. 
4-2-107 
DISTRICT CB - COMMUNITY BUSINESS 
Community business areas are to be primarily used as retail centers which serve a number of 
neighborhoods and are located where retail stores and services predominate. 
a. 
Uses by Right 
i. 
Child care facilities 
ii. 
Drive-up facilities 
iii. 
Entertainment and recreation facilities and uses 
iv. 
Food service facilities 
v. 
Hotels and motels 
vi. 
Offices 
vii. Retail, service and wholesale establishments, with outdoor storage. 
viii. Churches, temples or other places of worship 
ix. 
Any fabrication or processing use which does not emit noxious noise, smoke, 
odor, or dust beyond the confines of the property, does not emit pollutants to the 
soil and where all sales and storage of supplies and equipment are conducted 
within a building. 
x. 
Personal storage units within an existing building 
xi. 
Adult entertainment establishments operated in accordance with these regulations 
xii. Small Wind Energy Systems. (Setbacks shall be equal or greater to the total height 
of the small wind energy system.) 
b. 
Uses Requiring Board Approval 
i. 
The following uses may be permitted by the Board: 
ii. 
Bars, cocktail lounges, and liquor stores 
iii. 
Storage facilities 
iv. 
Transportation facilities 
v. 
Mixed-use residential developments 
vi. 
Employee living quarters 
vii. Fireworks stands 
viii. Other uses similar to those permitted in this district 

The Laramie County Land Use Regulations 
Amended October 4, 2022 
Page | 219 
 
 
 
c. 
Maximum Property Coverage 
Total building, parking, and outdoor storage area shall not exceed 85 percent of the 
property area. Best Management Practices for storm water management and open 
space design is encouraged. Characteristics such as community open space and pocket 
parks and connectivity to regional trails, including the Greater Cheyenne Greenway, are 
encouraged. 
d. 
Setbacks 
i. 
Accessory structure setbacks shall conform to Section 2-2-118 of this regulation. 
ii. 
All other setbacks – refer to table in Section 4-1-104. 
e. 
Height 
i. 
Buildings shall be limited to seventy-five (75) feet in height. 
ii. 
Board Approval is required for buildings over seventy-five (75) feet in height. 
f. 
Site Design 
All areas, including areas from right-of-way line to property line that are not covered by 
buildings, sidewalks, and parking area, shall be landscaped. 
g. 
Parking 
Parking requirements shall be in conformance with these regulations. 
h. 
Commercial Use Site Plan Requirements 
A County-approved site and landscape plan shall be required in accordance with 
Sections 2-2-133 and 2-2-134 of these regulations. 
Screening is required in conformance with site plan requirements for outdoor storage 
areas of items and equipment which are not for immediate use, sale or lease. Screening 
for outdoor storage areas shall be located behind the setback line. 
4-2-108 
DISTRICT LI - LIGHT INDUSTRIAL 
Light industrial areas are to be primarily developed for environmentally controlled manufacturing or 
intensive employment uses and may have accessory commercial or office uses. High quality site 
design, including the use of Best Management Practices for storm water management and 
reduction in the site’s carbon emissions and energy use are encouraged. 
a. 
Uses by Right 
i. 
Any industrial, manufacturing, fabrication, or processing use, associated offices 
and accessory commercial activities which does not emit noxious noise, smoke, 
odor, or dust, which does not emit pollutants to the soil and is confined to a 
building. 
ii. 
Child care facilities associated with other uses permitted in the district 
iii. 
Recreational facilities and uses associated with other uses permitted in the district 
iv. 
Retail uses 
v. 
Storage facilities or yards 
vi. 
Transportation facilities 
The Laramie County Land Use Regulations 
Amended October 4, 2022 
Page | 220 
 
 
 
vii. Warehousing 
viii. Wholesale uses 
ix. 
Office buildings 
x. 
Educational facilities 
b. 
Uses Requiring Board Approval 
The following uses may be permitted by the Board. If any of these uses are associated 
with a use by right, it will be considered accessory to that use and will not require special 
approval. 
i. 
Recycling uses 
ii. 
Other uses similar to those permitted in this district 
iii. 
Pet crematory 
iv. 
Kennels and pet boarding 
c. 
Maximum Building Coverage 
Total building, parking, and outdoor storage area shall not exceed 85 percent of the 
property area. 
d. 
Setbacks 
Accessory structure setbacks shall conform to Section 2-2-118 of this regulation. All 
other setbacks – refer to table in Section 4-1-104. 
e. 
Height 
i. 
Buildings shall be limited to seventy-five (75) feet in height. 
ii. 
Board Approval is required for buildings over seventy-five (75) feet in height. 
f. 
Site Design 
All areas, including areas from right-of-way line to property line that are not covered by 
buildings, sidewalks, and parking area, shall be landscaped. 
g. 
Parking 
Parking requirements shall be in conformance with these regulations. 
h. 
Commercial Use Site Plan Requirements 
A County-approved site and landscape plan shall be required in accordance with 
Sections 2-2-133 and 2-2-134 of these regulations. 
Screening is required in conformance with site plan requirements for outdoor storage 
areas of items and equipment which are not for immediate use, sale or lease. Screening 
for outdoor storage areas shall be located behind the setback line. 
4-2-109 
DISTRICT HI - HEAVY INDUSTRIAL 
Heavy industrial areas are to be primarily developed for those uses which should be isolated and 
buffered in order to protect both the community and the land use. High quality site design, including 
the use of Best Management Practices in storm water management and reduction in the site’s 
carbon emissions, are encouraged. 

The Laramie County Land Use Regulations 
Amended October 4, 2022 
Page | 221 
 
 
 
a. 
Uses by Right 
i. 
Any industrial, manufacturing, fabrication, or processing uses, including accessory 
office and commercial uses, which may emit noxious noise, smoke, odor, dust or 
pollutants. 
ii. 
Child care facilities associated with other uses permitted in the district 
iii. 
Railroad yards and maintenance buildings 
iv. 
Recreational facilities and uses associated with other uses permitted in the district 
v. 
Storage facilities or yards 
vi. 
Warehousing 
vii. Wholesaling 
b. 
Uses Requiring Board Approval 
The following uses may be permitted by the Board. If any of these uses are associated 
with a use by right, it will be considered accessory to that use, and will not require 
special approval. 
i. 
Salvage yards or junk yards 
ii. 
Recycling facilities 
iii. 
Other uses similar to those permitted in this district 
c. 
Maximum Building Coverage 
Total building, parking, and outdoor storage area shall not exceed 90 percent of the 
property area. 
d. 
Setbacks 
Accessory structure setbacks shall conform to Section 2-2-118 of this regulation. All 
other setbacks – refer to table in Section 4-1-104. 
e. 
Height 
i. 
Buildings shall be limited to one hundred (100) feet in height. 
ii. 
Board Approval is required for buildings over one hundred (100) feet in height. 
f. 
Site Design 
All areas, including areas from right-of-way line to property line, that are not covered by 
buildings, sidewalks, and parking area shall be landscaped. 
g. 
Parking 
Parking requirements shall be in conformance with these regulations. 
h. 
Commercial Use Site Plan Requirements 
A County-approved site and landscape plan shall be required in accordance with 
Sections 2-2-133 and 2-2-134 of these regulations. 
Screening is required in conformance with site plan requirements for outdoor storage 
areas of items and equipment which are not for immediate use, sale or lease. Screening 
for outdoor storage areas shall be located behind the setback line. 
The Laramie County Land Use Regulations 
Amended October 4, 2022 
Page | 222 
 
 
 
4-2-110 
DISTRICT P - PUBLIC 
This district is primarily for governmental buildings and government- sponsored uses where the 
activities conducted are directed to providing services to the public. It includes educational facilities 
and recreational areas. If the property or portions of the property with this classification are sold or 
transferred to a nongovernmental entity, the district classification shall be changed before the 
property can be used. 
a. 
Uses by Right 
i. 
Child care facilities associated with other uses permitted in the district 
ii. 
Educational facilities 
iii. 
Government offices 
iv. 
Hospitals 
v. 
Open space 
vi. 
Parks 
vii. Buildings and uses accessory to those allowed in this district, including uses such 
as restaurants, bars, cocktail lounges, etc. 
viii. Recreational facilities, including uses such as playing fields, tennis courts, 
swimming pools, golf courses, fairgrounds and model airplane or vehicle fields 
ix. 
Cemeteries 
b. 
Uses Requiring Board Approval 
The following uses may be permitted by the Board: 
i. 
Government buildings and structures not listed above. 
ii. 
Race tracks 
iii. 
Other uses similar to those permitted in this district 
c. 
Maximum Property Coverage 
Total building, parking, and outdoor storage area shall not exceed 85 percent of the 
property area. 
Best Management Practices for storm water management and open space design are 
encouraged. Characteristics such as community open space, pocket parks and 
connectivity to regional trails, including the Greater Cheyenne Greenway, are 
encouraged. 
d. 
Setbacks 
Accessory structure setbacks shall conform to Section 2-2-118 of this regulation. 
All other setbacks – refer to table in Section 4-1-104. 
e. 
Height 
i. 
Buildings shall be limited to seventy-five (75) feet in height. 
ii. 
Board Approval is required for buildings over seventy-five (75) feet in height. 

The Laramie County Land Use Regulations 
Amended October 4, 2022 
Page | 223 
 
 
 
f. 
Screening and Buffering 
Screening or buffering is required in conformance with Site Plan requirements for 
outdoor storage areas of items and equipment which are not for immediate use, sale or 
lease. These outdoor storage areas, including the screening or buffering, shall be 
located behind the setback line. 
g. 
Site Design 
All areas, including areas from right-of-way line to property line that are not covered by 
buildings, sidewalks, and parking area, shall be landscaped. 
h. 
Parking 
Parking requirements shall be in conformance with these regulations. 
i. 
Commercial Use Site Plan Requirements 
A County-approved site and landscape plan shall be required in accordance with 
Sections 2-2-133 and 2-2-134 of these regulations. 
4-2-111 
DISTRICT MU - MIXED USE 
Mixed use developing areas are to be used for a mix of residential and commercial uses. This 
district is intended to encourage rehabilitation and reuse of existing buildings in the established 
areas of the community. 
a. 
Uses by Right 
i. 
Single family, townhouse, duplex residential 
ii. 
Mixed use residential developments 
iii. 
Family child care homes 
iv. 
Home occupations 
v. 
Churches, temples or other places of worship 
vi. 
Offices 
vii. Multi-family residential 
viii. The retail sale of goods when the use is proposed in a building and the sale and 
storage of equipment and supplies are conducted within the building. 
ix. 
Child care facilities 
x. 
Assisted living facilities 
b. 
Uses Requiring Board Approval 
The following uses may be permitted by the Board: 
i. 
Food service facilities 
ii. 
Bars, cocktail lounges and liquor stores 
iii. 
Other uses similar to those permitted in this district 
iv. 
Entertainment facilities 
v. 
Outdoor storage in association with an approved use 
The Laramie County Land Use Regulations 
Amended October 4, 2022 
Page | 224 
 
 
 
c. 
Minimum Property Area 
i. 
Single-family: 6,000 square feet per unit 
ii. 
Townhouse/duplex: 3,000 square feet per unit 
iii. 
Multi-family: 1,860 square feet per unit 
d. 
Maximum Building Coverage 
i. 
Single-family: 60 percent of property area 
ii. 
Townhouse and duplexes: 50 percent of property area 
iii. 
Multi-family building and parking area: seventy (70) percent of property area 
iv. 
Total building and parking areas shall not exceed seventy five (75) percent of the 
total property area for nonresidential uses. 
v. 
Best Management Practices for storm water management and open space design 
are encouraged. Characteristics such as community open space, pocket parks 
and connectivity to regional trails, including the Greater Cheyenne Greenway, are 
encouraged. 
e. 
Setbacks 
Accessory structure setbacks shall conform to Section 2-2-118 of this regulation. All 
other setbacks – refer to table in Section 4-1-104. 
f. 
Height 
i. 
The maximum building height shall be thirty-five (35) feet. 
ii. 
Board Approval is required for buildings over thirty-five (35) feet in height. 
g. 
Site Design 
All areas, including areas from right-of-way line to property line that are not covered by 
buildings, sidewalks, and parking area, shall be landscaped. 
h. 
Parking 
Parking requirements shall be in conformance with these regulations. 
i. 
Commercial Use Site Plan Requirements 
A County-approved site and landscape plan shall be required in accordance with 
Sections 2-2-133 and 2-2-134 of these regulations. 
Screening is required in conformance with site plan requirements for outdoor storage 
areas of items and equipment which are not for immediate use, sale or lease. Screening 
for outdoor storage areas shall be located behind the setback line. 
4-2-112 
DISTRICT PUD - PLANNED UNIT DEVELOPMENT 
A planned unit development shall permit flexibility and creativity in site, building design and location 
in accordance with an approved plan and specific regulation which shall be written in a manner as 
to prevent adverse impacts and protect the public health, safety and welfare. Design excellence or 
the provision of outstanding public amenities shall be considered when establishing development 
standards. The planned unit development regulation shall be considered the zoning regulation for 
the property described in that regulation.

BE IT ORDAINED BY THE GOVERNING BODY OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE, 
WYOMING: 
 
 
Section 1.  That City staff has petitioned Governing Body for a zoning map amendment 
(“zone change”) for said property in accordance with Section 2.2.1, Zoning Map Amendment, of 
the Cheyenne Unified Development Code (“UDC”) in accordance with their 2022 goal to annex 
County pockets. 
 
 
Section 2.  That Section 2.2.8 of the UDC provides standards and procedures for zoning 
districts established on land or property currently without a City zone assignment. 
 
 
Section 3.  That on November 6, 2023, the City of Cheyenne Planning Commission 
conducted public hearings, reviewed the proposed zone changes that are the subject of this 
ordinance, and recommended approval of said zone changes. 
 
 
Section 4.  That the Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne finds that the zone change 
that is the subject of this ordinance meets the criteria specified in Section 2.2.8.d of the UDC.

classifications of the real properties described as:

MEMO 
To: 
Honorable Mayor Patrick Collins and Members of the City Council 
From: 
Seth Lloyd, Senior Planner 
Subject: A Text Amendment amending subsection 6.7.5.b of the Unified Development Code (UDC), 
changing windows requirements for multifamily residential development   
Date: 
November 27, 2023 
 
Background:  
The item for consideration is a UDC Text Amendment. This is approved by Ordinance.    
The proposed Governing Body review schedule is as follows: 
• 
Introduction and Referral  
11/27/2023 
• 
Public Services Committee  
12/5/2023 
• 
Second reading  
 
12/11/2023 
• 
Public Services Committee  
12/19/2023 
• 
Third and final reading   
12/21/2023 
 
Planning Commission Recommendation:  
The Planning Commission heard this item at their November 20, 2023 meeting and voted 3-0 to 
recommend the Governing Body approve this application.  
 
The Planning Commission originally heard this item in concert with building façade materials 
requirements, but divided the question and postponed the section regarding windows. This Ordinance 
reflects the windows section of the original suite of façade amendments. 
 
UDC 6.7.5.b.1 requires openings (defined in this section as transparent windows) on the ground floor of 
commercial, mixed-use, and urban loft buildings in certain zone districts. The design standards of 6.7 are 
generally applicable to when development is pulled closer to the street, and the windows requirement is 
an attempt to help activate the pedestrian space adjacent to the building. The windows are required on 
60%-90% of the ground-level façade. Staff has received feedback from developers that the windows 
portion of the design standards in 6.7 are not necessarily appropriate to multifamily development. The 
proposed text amendment essentially makes ground-floor residential areas exempt from the windows 
requirement. Should the Council wish to have some sort of minimum windows requirement, a smaller 
percentage might be appropriate. For example, UDC 6.6.4.b.2 (multifamily design) requires 15% 
windows on the entire façade, UDC 6.7.5.b.3 (a part of this section) allows upper level windows to 
occupy as little as 20% of the façade, and UDC 6.9.4.b (for suburban commercial buildings) allows 
arcades, awnings, or other such features to count for openings. 
 
 
 
  
  
  
  
  
  
  
  Planning and Development Department 
2101 O’Neil Avenue, Suite 202, Cheyenne WY 82001 
(Phone) 307-637-6282 (Fax) 307-637-6366 

The Planning Commission Staff report is attached and has not been changed. 
 
Attachments: 
1. Ordinance 
2. Planning Commission Staff Report 

ORDINANCE NO. ________ 
 
ENTITLED: "AN ORDINANCE AMENDING SUBSECTION 6.7.5.b OF THE UNIFIED 
DEVELOPMENT CODE (UDC), CHANGING BUILDING FAÇADE 
DESIGN REQUIREMENTS TO REDUCE REQUIRED WINDOWS ON 
URBAN LOFT MULTIFAMILY RESIDENTIAL AND MIXED-USE 
RESIDENTIAL DEVELOPMENT." 
 
 
BE IT ORDAINED BY THE GOVERNING BODY OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE, 
WYOMING: 
 
Section 1.  That the Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne finds that the text amendment 
that is the subject of this ordinance meets the criteria specified in Section 2.4.1.d of the Unified 
Development Code. 
 
Section 2.  That on November 20, 2023, the Planning Commission conducted a public 
hearing, reviewed the proposed text amendment, and recommended approval of said text 
amendment. 
 
Section 3.  That Subsection 6.7.5.b Facade Openings, of Subsection 6.7.5 Building Design, 
of Section 6.7 Small Scale Commercial and Mixed-use Design Standards, of Article 6 Design 
Regulations, of the Unified Development Code of the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, is hereby 
amended as set out in this section. 
 
b. Façade Openings. Each facade facing a street shall have openings at street level and upper 
levels according to the following: 
1. Between 60% and 90% of all street-level facades between 2’ and 10’ above grade shall 
be transparent with views to the interior of the building. No window starting at a level 
of greater than 3.5’ above the street level should be included in the calculation. Where 
the interior operation or program of the building warrants screening from the street, a 
closed display window of at least 2.5 feet deep may be substituted along up to 50% of 
the linear distance of the façade. Any section of a building with residential dwelling 
units on the floor that predominately covers the space between 2’ and 10’ above grade 
are exempt from this requirement in the areas where the residential units are located. 
2. No more than 20’ of building frontage shall exist at the street level without window or 
door openings. On secondary or support streets that are not the primary pedestrian and 
retail streets of the Center, the Director may approve facades with greater than 20’ of 
building frontage without window and door openings. 
3. Upper level openings shall occupy between 20% and 50% of the façade and consist of 
punched and regularly spaced openings to create a rhythm and pedestrian scale for the 
façade. 
4. Corner buildings with two street frontages may designate one frontage as the primary 
frontage; however, at least the first 30’ of secondary frontage from the corner shall 
meet the above minimum opening requirements.

unconstitutional or otherwise unlawful, such declaration shall not affect any of the remaining 
sections, paragraphs, sentences, clauses, and phrases. 
 
 
Section 5.  This ordinance shall be in full force and effect upon approval and publication.

October 16, 2023 
 
Planning and Development Office 
2101 O’Neil Avenue, Suite 202, Cheyenne WY 82001 
(Phone) 307-637-6282   (Fax) 307-637-6366 
 
 
 
 
PLANNING COMMISSION  
NOVEMBER 20, 2023 
STAFF REPORT  
 
REQUEST: 
 
A Text Amendment amending subsection 6.7.5.b changing building façade design 
requirements for multifamily residential development   
CASE NUMBER:  
PLTA-23-2 
PREPARED BY: 
Seth Lloyd, Senior Planner, Planning and Development Department 
 
 
RECOMMENDED MOTION:  
 
Move to recommend the Governing Body approve text amendments to the Unified Development Code 
subsection 6.7.5.b as outlined in this staff report and recommended by staff, noting the requests meets 
the review criteria of UDC 2.4.1.d.   
 
 
BACKGROUND: 
 
The City’s Unified Development Code (UDC) was adopted by the Governing Body on January 23, 2012, by 
Ordinance Number 3943 with an effective date of April 30, 2012.  The Unified Development Code is the 
regulatory document carrying out the vision of PlanCheyenne and is reviewed annually pursuant to UDC 
1.1.7 [Annual Review Requirement] to determine if any particular standard or regulation is timely, 
relevant and adequately addresses current market conditions. In addition to the annual review, the 
Planning and Development Director and City Engineer are authorized under UDC 2.4.1.b.3 to bring 
forward text amendments on their own initiative.  
 
City Staff brought forward a text amendment proposing changing various façade requirements for 
multifamily buildings. The question was divided and the ‘windows’ portion of the façade requirements 
amendment was postponed. This Staff Report is for the ‘windows’ portion of the multifamily-centric 
façade amendments. 
 
Multifamily buildings are called ‘Urban Lofts’ in certain scenarios, applying the design requirements of 6.7 
in these cases. Section 6.7 requires display windows on 60% to 90% of street-level façades. This proposed 
code change proposes removing the display window requirement for Urban Lofts (multifamily) and mixed-
use buildings where multifamily on the ground floor. Although this particular amendment was not 
requested by the Affordable Housing Task Force nor by any specific request by members of the public; 
however, Staff has run into this issue multiple times with development actions and felt that it was apropos 
to the request to make façade requirements make more sense for multifamily development. 
 
  
  
  
  
  
  
  
  Planning and Development Department 
2101 O’Neil Avenue, Suite 202, Cheyenne WY 82001 
(Phone) 307-637-6282 
(Fax) 307-637-6366 

PLTA-23-2  
 
 
October 16, 2023 
 
PROPOSED AMENDMENTS: 
Subsection 6.7.5.b Façade Openings  
(of 6.7.5 Building Design, of 6.7 Small Scale Commercial and Mixed-use Design Standards): 
 
b. Façade Openings. Each facade facing a street shall have openings at street level and upper levels 
according to the following: 
1. Between 60% and 90% of all street-level facades between 2’ and 10’ above grade shall be 
transparent with views to the interior of the building. No window starting at a level of 
greater than 3.5’ above the street level should be included in the calculation. Where the 
interior operation or program of the building warrants screening from the street, a closed 
display window of at least 2.5 feet deep may be substituted along up to 50% of the linear 
distance of the façade. Any section of a building with residential dwelling units on the floor 
that predominately covers the space between 2’ and 10’ above grade are exempt from this 
requirement in the areas where the residential units are located. 
2. No more than 20’ of building frontage shall exist at the street level without window or door 
openings. On secondary or support streets that are not the primary pedestrian and retail 
streets of the Center, the Director may approve facades with greater than 20’ of building 
frontage without window and door openings. 
3. Upper level openings shall occupy between 20% and 50% of the façade and consist of 
punched and regularly spaced openings to create a rhythm and pedestrian scale for the 
façade. 
4. Corner buildings with two street frontages may designate one frontage as the primary 
frontage; however, at least the first 30’ of secondary frontage from the corner shall meet 
the above minimum opening requirements. 
REVIEW CRITERIA: 
 
Review of a UDC text amendment shall be measured by the following three (3) criteria of Section 2.4.1.d: 
 
1. COMPREHENSIVE PLAN:  
“The amendment is in accordance with general goals and policies of the Comprehensive Plan, or 
is necessary to address an emerging issue not anticipated in the Comprehensive Plan and is not 
inconsistent with the plan.” 
 
The Comprehensive plan encourages the development of workforce housing and housing for 
Seniors (Principle 2). PlanCheyenne encourages workforce housing to be made of high-quality 
materials, and with architectural details that are functional and attractive (Policy 2.5.c). Another 
policy states that design standards should be created to address should be high-quality, but no 
specific materials or openings requirements are mentioned. 
    
2. STATE STATUTORY REQUIREMENTS:  
The amendment is consistent with the Wyoming Statutes, and specifically the Municipal Planning 
and Zoning statutes. The following Articles are all listed verbatim from Wyoming State Statute. 
 
WYO 15-1-601. 
Regulations; scope and purpose; uniformity within authorized districts; 
to follow plan; objectives. 
  
(a)  The governing body of any city or town, by ordinance, may: 

PLTA-23-2  
 
 
October 16, 2023 
 
(i)  Regulate and restrict the: 
  
 
(A)  Height, number of stories and size of buildings and other structures; 
(B)  Percentage of lot that may be occupied; 
  
 
(C)  Size of yards, courts and other open spaces; 
(D)  Density of population; and 
  
 
(E) Location and use of buildings, structures and land for trade, industry, 
residence or other purposes. 
  
(ii)  Establish setback building lines. 
  
(b)  The governing body may divide the city or town into districts of such number, shape and area 
as it deems necessary, and within those districts it may regulate and restrict the erection, 
construction, reconstruction, alteration, repair or use of buildings, structures or land. 
  
(c)  Regulations may differ from one (1) district to another but shall be uniform for each class or 
kind of buildings within a district. 
  
(d)  All regulations shall be made: 
(i)  In accordance with a comprehensive plan and designed to: 
  
 
(A)  Lessen congestion in the streets; 
  
 
(B)  Secure safety from fire, panic and other dangers; 
  
 
(C)  Promote health and general welfare; 
  
 
(D)  Provide adequate light and air; 
  
 
(E)  Prevent the overcrowding of land; 
  
 
(F)  Avoid undue concentration of population; and 
  
 
(G)  Facilitate adequate provisions for transportation, water, sewerage, schools, 
parks and other public requirements. 
(ii)  With reasonable consideration, among other things, of the character of the district 
and its peculiar suitability for particular uses; 
  
(iii)  With a view to conserving the value of buildings and encouraging the most 
appropriate use of land throughout the city or town; and 
(iv)  With consideration given to the historic integrity of certain neighborhoods or districts 
and a view to preserving, rehabilitating and maintaining historic properties and encouraging 
compatible uses within the neighborhoods or districts, but no regulation made to carry out the 
purposes of this paragraph is valid to the extent it constitutes an unconstitutional taking without 
compensation. 
 
Assessment: This amendment proposes modifying the Unified Development Code to modify 
sections of code in accordance with the general welfare and character of the community. The 
regulations and the modifications to the regulations are allowed by state statute. 
 
3. HEALTH, SAFETY, AND PUBLIC WELFARE:  
“The amendment promotes the general health, safety and public welfare.” 
 
This amendment would not adversely impact the general health, safety and public welfare of 
Cheyenne. While reducing construction costs, the amendment attempts to still assure the 
aesthetic values of the community are met. 
 
 
 
 

PLTA-23-2  
 
 
October 16, 2023 
 
 
 
CONCLUSION:  
 
Staff concludes that this text amendment meets the review criteria of UDC 2.4.1.d. 
 
 
STAFF RECOMMENDATION:  
 
Staff recommends approval of the text amendment to the Unified Development Code subsection 6.7.5.b 
as outlined in this staff report and recommended by staff, noting the requests meets the review criteria 
of UDC 2.4.1.d.

PLTA-23-3 
 
 
November 20, 2023 
 
Planning and Development Office 
2101 O’Neil Avenue, Suite 202, Cheyenne WY 82001 
(Phone) 307-637-6282   (Fax) 307-637-6366 
 
 
 
 
PLANNING COMMISSION  
NOVEMBER 20, 2023 
STAFF REPORT  
 
REQUEST: 
 
A Text Amendment amending the Unified Development Code Section 5.1.5. 
CASE NUMBER:  
PLTA-23-3 
PREPARED BY: 
Connor White, Planner II, Planning and Development Department 
 
 
RECOMMENDED MOTION:  
 
Move to recommend the Governing Body approve text amendments to the Unified Development Code 
Section 5.1.5 as outlined in this staff report and recommended by staff, noting the requests meet the 
review criteria of UDC 2.4.1.d.   
 
 
BACKGROUND: 
 
The City’s Unified Development Code (UDC) was adopted by the Governing Body on January 23, 2012, by 
Ordinance Number 3943 with an effective date of April 30, 2012.  The Unified Development Code is the 
regulatory document carrying out the vision of PlanCheyenne and is reviewed annually pursuant to UDC 
1.1.7 [Annual Review Requirement] to determine if any particular standard or regulation is timely, 
relevant and adequately addresses current market conditions. In addition to the annual review, the 
Planning and Development Director and City Engineer are authorized under UDC 2.4.1.b.3 to bring 
forward text amendments on their own initiative.  
 
The proposed text amendment is one of many small amendments that will be coming forward as staff 
looks at Article 5 and works on changes based on the Affordable Housing Task Force recommendations. 
One of the recommendations is to eliminate the minimum Lot area for residential properties. Staff 
reviewed the many Lot types that are within the UDC and found that the minimum Lot area for the 
following lots could be removed, Lot Types DD5, DD6, DD7, DD8, SD1, SD2, SD3, AD1, AD2, AD3 and the 
MD3.  
 
When staff reviews a Lot to determine its Lot type for setbacks, area is a factor, but we also look at the 
Lot frontage and the zone district it is located in. Removing the minimum area will not make determining 
the Lot type more challenging as there is still a minimum Lot frontage. Currently, certain Lot types are 
only allowed in certain zone districts so that will also become more of a factor when determining the Lot 
type for setbacks. 
 
  
  
  
  
  
  
  
  Planning and Development Department 
2101 O’Neil Avenue, Suite 202, Cheyenne WY 82001 
(Phone) 307-637-6282 
(Fax) 307-637-6366 

PLTA-23-3 
 
 
November 20, 2023 
 
Removing the minimum Lot area will not create problems with new subdivisions because there are 
frontage requirements and the setback requirements for each Lot type. A residential lot will still be 
required to be a certain size to allow it to develop and be a buildable lot. Removing the minimum lot area 
will also allow a mix of residential lots and allow more duplex and townhouse lots as long as the minimum 
lot frontage is met.  
 
There have been several Housing Studies and recommendations that have come out of them for the State 
of Wyoming. The 2023 Housing Study that was completed for Wyoming recommends that minimum lot 
size be changed. It states that “Smaller lot sizes allow for more single-family homes to be built and to be 
built more densely, which means land acquisition won’t be nearly as onerous. Any opportunities for 
homeownership will be preferred to renting, so the best options under current conditions are condos and 
townhomes.” In another study it states “Lot size and density regulations may be the most powerful tool 
available to cities to influence the price of new housing development. Allowing more density allows land 
costs to be spread across more units, which reduces the minimum sale price or monthly rent needed to 
make a project financially feasible.”  Staff believes that the proposed text amendment would be beneficial 
for the community and provide additional housing options. This text amendment would also allow for 
new neighborhoods to be denser and allow for more residential units.       
 
PROPOSED AMENDMENTS: 
The proposed amendments are to remove the minimum Lot area requirements for Lot Types DD5, DD6, 
DD7, DD8, SD1, SD2, SD3, AD1, AD2, AD3 and the MD3. See the attached exhibit for the proposed 
amendments. 
 
REVIEW CRITERIA: 
 
Review of a UDC text amendment shall be measured by the following three (3) criteria of Section 2.4.1.d: 
 
1. COMPREHENSIVE PLAN:  
“The amendment is in accordance with general goals and policies of the Comprehensive Plan, or 
is necessary to address an emerging issue not anticipated in the Comprehensive Plan and is not 
inconsistent with the plan.” 
 
The proposed amendment is in compliance with multiple goals and policies such as: Policy 1.1.B 
Flexibility to respond to market demands, 1.4.A Infill Opportunities, Policy2.1.B Transition 
between existing and new neighborhoods, Policy 2.3.A Mix of Housing Types, to name a few. By 
removing the minimum lot size there will be more mixes of residential lots. Lot frontage will 
become the determining factor on how a neighborhood looks at the street level.     
    
2. STATE STATUTORY REQUIREMENTS:  
The amendment is consistent with the Wyoming Statutes, and specifically the Municipal Planning 
and Zoning statutes. The following Articles are all listed verbatim from Wyoming State Statute. 
 
WYO 15-1-601. 
The governing body of any city or town, by ordinance, may: 
 
(i)  Regulate and restrict the: 
(A)  Height, number of stories and size of buildings and 
other structures; 
(B)  Percentage of lot that may be occupied; 
(C)  Size of yards, courts and other open spaces; 

PLTA-23-3 
 
 
November 20, 2023 
 
(D)  Density of population; and 
(E)  Location and use of buildings, structures and land for 
trade, industry, residence or other purposes. 
 (ii)  Establish setback building lines. 
 
Assessment: This amendment proposes modifying the Unified Development Code to eliminate 
the minimum Lot area required for a number of residential Lot types. This does not affect setback 
requirements.  
 
3. HEALTH, SAFETY, AND PUBLIC WELFARE:  
“The amendment promotes the general health, safety and public welfare.” 
 
This amendment would allow for lots to range more in size and to allow the review of lots to be 
easier. When reviewing lots it will be based on minimum lot frontage and the zone district the lot 
is in. This amendment does not affect the general health, safety, and public welfare. 
 
 
CONCLUSION:  
 
Staff concludes that this text amendment meets the review criteria of UDC 2.4.1.d. 
 
 
STAFF RECOMMENDATION:  
 
Staff recommends approval of text amendments to the Unified Development Code Section 5.1.5 as 
outlined in this staff report and recommended by staff, noting the requests meet the review criteria of 
UDC 2.4.1.d.

MEMO 
To: 
Honorable Mayor Patrick Collins and Members of the City Council 
From: 
Connor White, Planner II 
Subject: PLTA-23-4 – Amending UDC Section 5.1.5  
Date: 
November 27, 2023 
 
Background:  
The item for consideration is a UDC Text Amendment. This is approved by Ordinance.    
 
The proposed Governing Body review schedule is as follows: 
• 
Introduction and Referral  
11/27/2023 
• 
Public Services Committee  
12/05/2023 
• 
Second reading  
 
12/11/2023 
• 
Public Services Committee  
12/19/2023 
• 
Third and final reading   
12/21/2023 
 
Planning Commission Recommendation:  
The Planning Commission heard this item at their November 20, 2023, meeting and voted 4-0 to 
recommend the Governing Body approve the proposed text amendment.  
 
The Planning Commission Staff report is attached and has not been changed.  
 
Attachments: 
1. Ordinance 
2. Planning Commission Staff Report 
  
  
  
  
  
  
  
  Planning and Development Department 
2101 O’Neil Avenue, Suite 202, Cheyenne WY 82001 
(Phone) 307-637-6282 (Fax) 307-637-6366 

ORDINANCE NO. ________ 
 
ENTITLED: "AN ORDINANCE AMENDING SECTION 5.1.5 OF THE UNIFIED 
DEVELOPMENT 
CODE 
(UDC), 
REMOVING 
DENSITY 
REQUIREMENTS FOR MULTI-DWELLING BUILDINGS." 
 
 
BE IT ORDAINED BY THE GOVERNING BODY OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE, 
WYOMING: 
 
Section 1.  That the Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne finds that the text amendment 
that is the subject of this ordinance meets the criteria specified in Section 2.4.1.d of the Unified 
Development Code. 
 
Section 2.  That on November 20, 2023, the Planning Commission conducted a public 
hearing, reviewed the proposed text amendment, and recommended approval of said text 
amendment. 
 
Section 3.  That Section 5.1.5, Residential Buildings and Lot Types, of Article 5, Zoning 
Regulations, General Provisions, of the Unified Development Code of the City of Cheyenne, 
Wyoming, is hereby amended to remove the density requirement for Lot Types MD1, MD2, and 
MD3.  
 
Section 4.  Sections, paragraphs, sentences, clauses, and phrases of this ordinance are 
severable. If any section, paragraph, sentence, clause, or phrase of this ordinance is declared 
unconstitutional or otherwise unlawful, such declaration shall not affect any of the remaining 
sections, paragraphs, sentences, clauses, and phrases. 
 
 
Section 5.  This ordinance shall be in full force and effect upon approval and publication.

SECOND READING:

(SEAL)

KRISTINA F. JONES, CITY CLERK 
 
Published:_____________________ 
Wyoming Tribune-Eagle 
11:52 am, Nov 21 2023

PLTA-23-4 
 
 
November 20, 2023 
 
Planning and Development Office 
2101 O’Neil Avenue, Suite 202, Cheyenne WY 82001 
(Phone) 307-637-6282   (Fax) 307-637-6366 
 
 
 
 
PLANNING COMMISSION  
NOVEMBER 20, 2023 
STAFF REPORT  
 
REQUEST: 
 
A Text Amendment amending the Unified Development Code Section 5.1.5.d and 
6.2.4.a, Table 6-2. 
CASE NUMBER:  
PLTA-23-4 
PREPARED BY: 
Connor White, Planner II, Planning and Development Department 
 
 
RECOMMENDED MOTION:  
 
Motion 1: 
Move to recommend the Governing Body approve text amendments to the Unified Development Code 
Section 5.1.5.d as outlined in this staff report and recommended by staff, noting the request meets the 
review criteria of UDC 2.4.1.d.   
 
Motion 2: 
Move to recommend the Governing Body approve text amendments to the Unified Development Code 
Section 6.2.4.a, Table 6-2 as outlined in this staff report and recommended by staff, noting the request 
meets the review criteria of UDC 2.4.1.d.   
 
 
 
BACKGROUND: 
 
The City’s Unified Development Code (UDC) was adopted by the Governing Body on January 23, 2012, by 
Ordinance Number 3943 with an effective date of April 30, 2012.  The Unified Development Code is the 
regulatory document carrying out the vision of PlanCheyenne and is reviewed annually pursuant to UDC 
1.1.7 [Annual Review Requirement] to determine if any particular standard or regulation is timely, 
relevant and adequately addresses current market conditions. In addition to the annual review, the 
Planning and Development Director and City Engineer are authorized under UDC 2.4.1.b.3 to bring 
forward text amendments on their own initiative.  
 
The proposed text amendments are two of many small amendments that will be coming forward as staff 
looks at Article 5 and works on changes based on the Affordable Housing Task Force recommendations. 
The first proposed text amendment is to remove the density requirement for multi-family lots. Currently 
all multi-family lots have a density requirement of 1 dwelling / 1,600 square feet and 1 dwelling / 1,000 
square feet, if over 3 stories. By removing the density requirement, it would allow for more residential 
units to be provided as long as all other UDC requirements are met. There is a requirement that any 
  
  
  
  
  
  
  
  Planning and Development Department 
2101 O’Neil Avenue, Suite 202, Cheyenne WY 82001 
(Phone) 307-637-6282 
(Fax) 307-637-6366 

PLTA-23-4 
 
 
November 20, 2023 
 
building over 3 stories must request a conditional use and can only go up to 5 stories. Removing the 
density requirement would not change this requirement. Other factors that would still limit the number 
of units is setbacks, percentage of landscaping required, impervious area, and parking.  
 
The second proposed amendment is to make some changes to parking requirements for multi-family 
development. One of the changes proposed is to allow 0.5-spaces for studio or one-bedroom units. This 
number is proposed from a study called Breaking the Code: Aligning Zoning Code with Housing Goals 
which is a toolkit created for Wyoming Communities. By adding this parking requirement to the code, it 
would provide an incentive for studio and one bedroom apartments which are missing within the 
community.  
 
Staff also proposes adding in a parking requirement for age restricted senior housing that reduces the 
parking down to 1 space per unit rather than the 1.5 spaces per unit under current requirements. Staff 
have been provided with studies by applicants justifying the reduction down to 1 space per unit and 
several senior housing developments through the community have developed with 1 space per unit and 
functioning just fine at this reduction. With these additions, the required parking for all other types of 
multi-family and all other number of units will still be required at 1.5 spaces.      
 
PROPOSED AMENDMENTS: 
The proposed amendments are to remove the density requirement from the MD1, MD2 and MD3 Lot 
Types. See the attached exhibit for the proposed amendments. 
 
The second proposed amendment is to modify the parking requirements for multi-family developments 
to the following: 
 
0.5 space per unit for studio or one-bedroom 
1 space per unit for age restricted senior housing 
1.5 spaces per unit for all other unit types 
 
REVIEW CRITERIA: 
 
Review of a UDC text amendment shall be measured by the following three (3) criteria of Section 2.4.1.d: 
 
1. COMPREHENSIVE PLAN:  
“The amendment is in accordance with general goals and policies of the Comprehensive Plan, or 
is necessary to address an emerging issue not anticipated in the Comprehensive Plan and is not 
inconsistent with the plan.” 
 
The proposed amendment is in compliance with multiple goals and policies such as: Policy 1.1.B 
Flexibility to respond to market demands, 1.4.A Infill Opportunities, 2.5.A Community Housing 
needs met, Policy 2.3.A Mix of Housing Types, and 2.5.B Workforce Housing throughout the 
community, to name a few. The proposed text amendments incentivize the development of more 
units throughout the community and hopefully provide incentivize the development of more 
studio and one-bedroom apartments.     
    
2. STATE STATUTORY REQUIREMENTS:  
The amendment is consistent with the Wyoming Statutes, and specifically the Municipal Planning 
and Zoning statutes. The following Articles are all listed verbatim from Wyoming State Statute. 
 

PLTA-23-4 
 
 
November 20, 2023 
 
WYO 15-1-601. 
The governing body of any city or town, by ordinance, may: 
 
(i)  Regulate and restrict the: 
(A)  Height, number of stories and size of buildings and 
other structures; 
(B)  Percentage of lot that may be occupied; 
(C)  Size of yards, courts and other open spaces; 
(D)  Density of population; and 
(E)  Location and use of buildings, structures and land for 
trade, industry, residence or other purposes. 
 (ii)  Establish setback building lines. 
 
Assessment: This amendment proposes modifying the Unified Development Code to remove the 
density requirement for multi-family development and change the parking requirements for 
multi-family development.  
 
3. HEALTH, SAFETY, AND PUBLIC WELFARE:  
“The amendment promotes the general health, safety and public welfare.” 
 
The proposed amendments will reduce the number of barriers to building multi-family 
developments and hopefully incentivize the construction of more studio and one-bedroom 
apartments which are missing within the community.  
 
 
CONCLUSION:  
 
Staff concludes that both proposed text amendments meet the review criteria of UDC 2.4.1.d. 
 
 
STAFF RECOMMENDATION:  
 
Staff recommends approval of text amendments to the Unified Development Code Section 5.1.5.d as 
outlined in this staff report and recommended by staff, noting the request meets the review criteria of 
UDC 2.4.1.d.   
 
Staff recommends approval of text amendments to the Unified Development Code Section 6.2.4.a, 
Table 6-2 as outlined in this staff report and recommended by staff, noting the request meets the review 
criteria of UDC 2.4.1.d.   
 
 
ADDITIONAL DOCUMENTS:  
 
Housing Studies: 
Breaking the Code: Aligning Zoning Code with Housing Goals 
2023 Housing Study 
 
Attachments: 
Exhibit A – Removal of the density requirement 
Exhibit B – Parking requirement changes

ORDINANCE NO. ________ 
 
ENTITLED: "AN ORDINANCE AMENDING SECTION 6.2.4 OF THE UNIFIED 
DEVELOPMENT CODE (UDC), AMENDING TABLE 6-2, REQUIRED 
AUTOMOBILE PARKING FOR MULTI-FAMILY DEVELOPMENTS." 
 
 
BE IT ORDAINED BY THE GOVERNING BODY OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE, 
WYOMING: 
 
Section 1.  That the Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne finds that the text amendment 
that is the subject of this ordinance meets the criteria specified in Section 2.4.1.d of the Unified 
Development Code. 
 
Section 2.  That on November 20, 2023, the Planning Commission conducted a public 
hearing, reviewed the proposed text amendment, and recommended approval of said text 
amendment. 
 
Section 3.  That Table 6-2, Required Automobile Parking, of Article 6, Design 
Regulations, Parking, Lot Access and Circulation, of the Unified Development Code of the City 
of Cheyenne, Wyoming, is hereby amended changing parking spaces required for “Residential: all 
other” to read as follows: 
 
0.5 space per unit for studio or one bedroom 
1 space per unit for age restricted senior housing 
1.5 spaces per unit for all other unit types 
 
Section 4.  Sections, paragraphs, sentences, clauses, and phrases of this ordinance are 
severable. If any section, paragraph, sentence, clause, or phrase of this ordinance is declared 
unconstitutional or otherwise unlawful, such declaration shall not affect any of the remaining 
sections, paragraphs, sentences, clauses, and phrases. 
 
 
Section 5.  This ordinance shall be in full force and effect upon approval and publication.

PLTA-23-4 
 
 
November 20, 2023 
 
Planning and Development Office 
2101 O’Neil Avenue, Suite 202, Cheyenne WY 82001 
(Phone) 307-637-6282   (Fax) 307-637-6366 
 
 
 
 
PLANNING COMMISSION  
NOVEMBER 20, 2023 
STAFF REPORT  
 
REQUEST: 
 
A Text Amendment amending the Unified Development Code Section 5.1.5.d and 
6.2.4.a, Table 6-2. 
CASE NUMBER:  
PLTA-23-4 
PREPARED BY: 
Connor White, Planner II, Planning and Development Department 
 
 
RECOMMENDED MOTION:  
 
Motion 1: 
Move to recommend the Governing Body approve text amendments to the Unified Development Code 
Section 5.1.5.d as outlined in this staff report and recommended by staff, noting the request meets the 
review criteria of UDC 2.4.1.d.   
 
Motion 2: 
Move to recommend the Governing Body approve text amendments to the Unified Development Code 
Section 6.2.4.a, Table 6-2 as outlined in this staff report and recommended by staff, noting the request 
meets the review criteria of UDC 2.4.1.d.   
 
 
 
BACKGROUND: 
 
The City’s Unified Development Code (UDC) was adopted by the Governing Body on January 23, 2012, by 
Ordinance Number 3943 with an effective date of April 30, 2012.  The Unified Development Code is the 
regulatory document carrying out the vision of PlanCheyenne and is reviewed annually pursuant to UDC 
1.1.7 [Annual Review Requirement] to determine if any particular standard or regulation is timely, 
relevant and adequately addresses current market conditions. In addition to the annual review, the 
Planning and Development Director and City Engineer are authorized under UDC 2.4.1.b.3 to bring 
forward text amendments on their own initiative.  
 
The proposed text amendments are two of many small amendments that will be coming forward as staff 
looks at Article 5 and works on changes based on the Affordable Housing Task Force recommendations. 
The first proposed text amendment is to remove the density requirement for multi-family lots. Currently 
all multi-family lots have a density requirement of 1 dwelling / 1,600 square feet and 1 dwelling / 1,000 
square feet, if over 3 stories. By removing the density requirement, it would allow for more residential 
units to be provided as long as all other UDC requirements are met. There is a requirement that any 
  
  
  
  
  
  
  
  Planning and Development Department 
2101 O’Neil Avenue, Suite 202, Cheyenne WY 82001 
(Phone) 307-637-6282 
(Fax) 307-637-6366 

PLTA-23-4 
 
 
November 20, 2023 
 
building over 3 stories must request a conditional use and can only go up to 5 stories. Removing the 
density requirement would not change this requirement. Other factors that would still limit the number 
of units is setbacks, percentage of landscaping required, impervious area, and parking.  
 
The second proposed amendment is to make some changes to parking requirements for multi-family 
development. One of the changes proposed is to allow 0.5-spaces for studio or one-bedroom units. This 
number is proposed from a study called Breaking the Code: Aligning Zoning Code with Housing Goals 
which is a toolkit created for Wyoming Communities. By adding this parking requirement to the code, it 
would provide an incentive for studio and one bedroom apartments which are missing within the 
community.  
 
Staff also proposes adding in a parking requirement for age restricted senior housing that reduces the 
parking down to 1 space per unit rather than the 1.5 spaces per unit under current requirements. Staff 
have been provided with studies by applicants justifying the reduction down to 1 space per unit and 
several senior housing developments through the community have developed with 1 space per unit and 
functioning just fine at this reduction. With these additions, the required parking for all other types of 
multi-family and all other number of units will still be required at 1.5 spaces.      
 
PROPOSED AMENDMENTS: 
The proposed amendments are to remove the density requirement from the MD1, MD2 and MD3 Lot 
Types. See the attached exhibit for the proposed amendments. 
 
The second proposed amendment is to modify the parking requirements for multi-family developments 
to the following: 
 
0.5 space per unit for studio or one-bedroom 
1 space per unit for age restricted senior housing 
1.5 spaces per unit for all other unit types 
 
REVIEW CRITERIA: 
 
Review of a UDC text amendment shall be measured by the following three (3) criteria of Section 2.4.1.d: 
 
1. COMPREHENSIVE PLAN:  
“The amendment is in accordance with general goals and policies of the Comprehensive Plan, or 
is necessary to address an emerging issue not anticipated in the Comprehensive Plan and is not 
inconsistent with the plan.” 
 
The proposed amendment is in compliance with multiple goals and policies such as: Policy 1.1.B 
Flexibility to respond to market demands, 1.4.A Infill Opportunities, 2.5.A Community Housing 
needs met, Policy 2.3.A Mix of Housing Types, and 2.5.B Workforce Housing throughout the 
community, to name a few. The proposed text amendments incentivize the development of more 
units throughout the community and hopefully provide incentivize the development of more 
studio and one-bedroom apartments.     
    
2. STATE STATUTORY REQUIREMENTS:  
The amendment is consistent with the Wyoming Statutes, and specifically the Municipal Planning 
and Zoning statutes. The following Articles are all listed verbatim from Wyoming State Statute. 
 

PLTA-23-4 
 
 
November 20, 2023 
 
WYO 15-1-601. 
The governing body of any city or town, by ordinance, may: 
 
(i)  Regulate and restrict the: 
(A)  Height, number of stories and size of buildings and 
other structures; 
(B)  Percentage of lot that may be occupied; 
(C)  Size of yards, courts and other open spaces; 
(D)  Density of population; and 
(E)  Location and use of buildings, structures and land for 
trade, industry, residence or other purposes. 
 (ii)  Establish setback building lines. 
 
Assessment: This amendment proposes modifying the Unified Development Code to remove the 
density requirement for multi-family development and change the parking requirements for 
multi-family development.  
 
3. HEALTH, SAFETY, AND PUBLIC WELFARE:  
“The amendment promotes the general health, safety and public welfare.” 
 
The proposed amendments will reduce the number of barriers to building multi-family 
developments and hopefully incentivize the construction of more studio and one-bedroom 
apartments which are missing within the community.  
 
 
CONCLUSION:  
 
Staff concludes that both proposed text amendments meet the review criteria of UDC 2.4.1.d. 
 
 
STAFF RECOMMENDATION:  
 
Staff recommends approval of text amendments to the Unified Development Code Section 5.1.5.d as 
outlined in this staff report and recommended by staff, noting the request meets the review criteria of 
UDC 2.4.1.d.   
 
Staff recommends approval of text amendments to the Unified Development Code Section 6.2.4.a, 
Table 6-2 as outlined in this staff report and recommended by staff, noting the request meets the review 
criteria of UDC 2.4.1.d.   
 
 
ADDITIONAL DOCUMENTS:  
 
Housing Studies: 
Breaking the Code: Aligning Zoning Code with Housing Goals 
2023 Housing Study 
 
Attachments: 
Exhibit A – Removal of the density requirement 
Exhibit B – Parking requirement changes
